## Introduction to Forecasting & Planning
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 11*

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Introduction to Forecasting & Planning 

Forecasting & Planning is a budgeting, planning, and forecasting solution used to map out an organization’s 

short-term and long-term financial goals by analyzing past and present data trends. 

This application allows you to create, monitor, and manage budgets or forecasts for corporate entities and then 

review the current performance of the budget against the last or current year’s budget or actuals. You can 

complete tasks such as: 

• 
Adding and modifying budget details, budget periods, and budget amounts. 

• 
Creating working budgets to serve as drafts before committing budget figures. 

• 
Setting up payroll definitions to run payroll calculations in a working budget. 

• 
Running reports to analyze budget and account information.  

Use the Forecasting & Planning Workbench (on page 7) to search, compare, and modify budgets from a central 

location. Use the Payroll Workbench (on page 248) to create and manage your company’s payroll(s) and publish 

their budgeted amounts to a working budget. 

In This Book

### Forecasting & Planning Setup Tab
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 14*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Forecasting & Planning Setup Tab 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup`


This section describes how to configure dimensions and segments to use with Forecasting & 

Planning. You must have administrator-level privileges to access the Setup tab. 

• 
Dimensions (on page 329): Use dimensions to collect more information about your business 

from one or more categories. Forecasting & Planning supports the following dimensions: 

• 
Vendors 

• 
Customers 

• 
Employees 

• 
Departments 

• 
Locations 
Forecasting & Planning uses existing dimension data that you have added using other applications in the RealPage Financial Suite. These dimension menu options allow you to add and manage the data from this application as well.

• 
Segments (on page 405): Segments are like custom-designed dimensions. Each company 

can set up segments for the data that it wants to track. Like dimensions, you can filter 

reports by segment.

## Using Forecasting & Planning
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 15*

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Using Forecasting & Planning 

Use the Forecasting & Planning application to create, manage, and monitor a budget forecast, and to compare 

your company’s current performance with last year’s actuals and current year targets. The application offers a 

workbench where you can search and change budgets on the fly and compare budget values with previous years’ 

budgets and actuals. 

This chapter describes the features and functions on the All tab of the Forecasting & Planning application. 

The application does require some setup. If you haven’t set up Forecasting & Planning, then see Forecasting & 

Planning Setup (on page 323).

#### Forecasting & Planning Workbench Page
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 18*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Forecasting & Planning Workbench Page 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Workbench`


The Workbench page is divided into two sections: 

• 
Filters: (on page 9) Use this section to filter the list of accounts in the workbench by specific 

criteria.  

• 
List: (on page 10) Use this list to view and manage working budgets by account. 
The list contents depends on whetheryou are viewing the working budget or comparing the budget. When viewing the working budget, the list contains the G/L accounts and account groups and the amounts budgeted per month/year.

By default, the accounts for all reporting periods are displayed. You can customize the columns 

and how the view is grouped using the Columns menu to the right of the list. 

---



#### Forecasting & Planning Workbench Filters
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 19*

Chapter 1: Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Forecasting & Planning Workbench Filters 

The Filters section is where you load the working budget and select the location. The Budget ID 

and Location fields are required fields in the Filter section. After selecting the two options, click 

Apply to display a list of accounts for the working budget. 

You can filter by: 

• 
Account: Select an account when you want to see one G/L account in the workbench list. 

• 
Dimensions: Select one of your dimensions to filter by department, customer, or employee. 

• 
Segments: Select one or more segments to filter the list by the selected segments. 

You can also: 

• 
Compare the working budget with another budget or the actuals of a specific year. 

• 
Filter the list to show all accounts, show only accounts with budget data, or show only 

accounts with no budget data. 

Click Apply to apply your filter selections to the list. You can click the Clear button to clear all 

settings, including the working budget and location, allowing you to start over with a new 

working budget or location. 

---



#### Forecasting & Planning Workbench List
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 20*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Forecasting & Planning Workbench List 

You can display the monthly budget figures for each G/L account in the workbench list or 

compare the actual or budgeted totals between two working budgets. 

Displaying Monthly Budget Amounts in the Workbench 

To display the working budget amounts by month: 

• 
In the Filters section, set the Display columns by field to Monthly, and then click Apply. The 

workbench list displays budget figures for the property and time frame that you selected.  

Working Budget Columns 

Group 
This column displays the name of the account group. 

Action 
Click an action link for an account: 

• 
Edit: Click to edit an account. 

• 
View: Click to view the details of an account. 

(Month-Year) 
This column shows the amount budgeted for the month and year. 

Comparing Working Budgets in the Workbench 

To compare two working budgets: 

1. 
Set Display columns by to Totals. 

2. 
In Compare with, select Budget or Actual. 

3. 
Type the desired year in Compare with Year. 

4. 
Click the Apply button. The workbench list displays the working budget figures and figures 

from actuals or another budget for the selected property and year. 

---


<!-- Page 21 -->
Chapter 1: Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Comparison Columns 

Account 
This is the G/L account. 

(Working budget name) - 

year 

This column contains the amount for the G/L account in the 

working budget and the year. 

(Comparison budget) - year This column contains the amount for the G/L account in the 

budget that you are comparing with, and the year. 

This column appears when you select Budget from the Compare 

with field. 

Actuals - year 
This column contains the actual amounts for each G/L account for 

the specified year. 

This column appears when you select Actuals from the Compare 

with field. 

Variance 
This column shows the amount by which the two budgets or 

actuals varied. 

Variance % 
This column shows the percentage difference between the two 

budgets or budget and actuals, per G/L account.

##### Comparing Two Budgets in the Workbench
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 22*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Comparing Two Budgets in the Workbench 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Workbench`


To display a comparison in the workbench: 

1. 
In the Display columns by drop-down list, select Totals. 

2. 
In the Compare with drop-down list, select Budget or Actual. 

3. 
Type the desired year in the Compare with Year field. 

4. 
Click the Apply button. The workbench list displays the working budget amounts, and 

amounts from actuals or another budget for the selected property and year.

##### Comparing the Working Budget with Another Budget or with Actuals
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 23*

Chapter 1: Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Comparing the Working Budget with Another Budget or with Actuals 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Workbench`


After loading the desired working budget, you can compare it to another budget or to actuals. 

 To compare the working budget to another budget: 

1. 
In the Compare with drop-down list, select Budget. 

2. 
In the Compare with year field, type the desired year to compare with. 

3. 
Click the Apply button. 

To compare the working budget with actuals: 

1. 
In the Compare with drop-down list, select Actual. 

2. 
In the Compare with year drop-down list, type the desired year of the actuals that you want 

to compare. 

3. 
Click the Apply button.

Workbench Features 

The workbench is a central location where you can monitor and manage transactions. The 

workbench offers controls that allow you to: 

• 
View all columns available in Accounting 

• 
Use SmartGrid technology to: 

• 
Rearrange the columns 

• 
Customize and save which columns you see by default 

• 
Group and ungroup transactions based on different fields, like vendor or property 

• 
Apply operators to columns 

• 
Multi-select different filters for the following: 

• 
Property 

• 
Transaction type 

• 
Status 

• 
Submitted by 

• 
Next approver 

• 
Units 

• 
Save filter settings as a view and then reapply the view any time that you return to the 

workbench 

• 
Retain filters when switching between transaction types to alleviate selecting and applying 

the same filters again on both Approval and Spend Management Workbenches 

• 
Select the option to include future orders 

• 
Export the data in the workbench to an Excel file

rearrange their order in the workbench.

d) Drag a column into the Values box to apply an operator to the column’s data.

Applying Mathematical Operators to Column Values

##### Rearranging Columns
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 28*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

The Amount column defaults to the sum ([column name]) operator: 

Click Close to discard the value operator.

2. 
Click the item to open the operator menu so that you can change the operator if needed. 

Available operators include: 

• 
Avg: Calculates the average of the numbers in a column. 

• 
Count: Counts the number of items in the column, or the number of elements in the 

collection. 

• 
First: Finds the first element in the column. 

• 
Last: Finds the last element in the column. 

• 
Max: Finds the largest number in the column. 

• 
Min: Finds the smallest number in the column. 

• 
Sum: Add the values in the column. The field can be a currency field or a numeric field. 

Rearranging Columns 

To rearrange columns in the workbench, drag a column header to a new spot in the list.

Using Column Menus 

All workbench columns have a menu with additional options. Roll over the column header to 

display the 
 Options icon. If you do not see the menu, expand the width of the column (by 

dragging the column edge on the header bar) and try again. 

Using Column Options 

Click the Options 
 icon to open the column menu. The default tab allows you to: 

• 
Pin column: Affix the column to the left or right side of the list. Select No pin to return the 

column to its sort order. 

• 
Autosize this column/Autosize all columns: Have the system resize the column or all 

columns based on the columns’ contents. 

• 
Group or Ungroup: Group the list by the selected column or (if grouped) ungroup the list 

by the selected column. Only some columns can be grouped. 

• 
Reset columns: Reset the columns to their default settings. 

• 
Expand all/Collapse all: Expand or collapse the column. This option appears only when the 

list is grouped by a column. 

Filtering Columns 

Some columns, such as Property name and Vendor name, have an additional 
 tab for filtering 

column data. From this tab, you can:

Search columns by name as you type.

##### Saving a Custom Workbench View
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 32*

Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Saving a Custom Workbench View 

You can save your workbench filters and selections as a view for settings that you use regularly. 

After saving a view, you can save it as your default view, or load it from the Apply view 

drop-down list.  

Creating a View 

To save your workbench settings as a view: 

1. 
In the workbench, set up the filters that you want to save. 

• 
Select a transaction type. 

• 
Select a date, property, or vendor that you frequently access. 

2. 
Click the Save view as button. 

3. 
Type a name for these selections, such as “A/P invoices for Alpha Properties.” 

4. 
If you want the view to be applied whenever you open the workbench, select the Default 

check box. You can also apply the view as needed by selecting it in the Apply view 

drop-down list. 

5. 
Click the Save button. 
You can also manage the views of the Approvals workbench in the Accounts Payable application by using the Approval Workbench Viewsoption.

Updating Views 

1. 
In the workbench, select the view that you want to change from the Apply view drop-down 

list. 

2. 
Make the desired changes to the filters and settings. 

3. 
Click the Update View button.

##### Exporting from a Workbench
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 33*

Chapter 1: Forecasting & Planning Workbench 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Exporting from a Workbench 

To export from a workbench: 

1. 
Right-click any data in the workbench to display the export controls menu. 

2. 
Click the Export button, and then select the file format that you want the system to use to 

export the data. 

• 
CSV Export: This format makes it easy to move the data from one program to another. 

You can use files that you save in the CSV format with any spreadsheet program. After 

selecting this option, the system exports the list as a CSV file. 

• 
Excel Export (.xlsx): After selecting this option, the system exports the list in Excel’s 

native format. 

• 
Excel Export (.xml): After selecting this option, the system exports the list in XML 

format. 
The location of the exported file depends on your browser’s default download functionality.

All three of these export formats export the entire list, including these links: 

• 
Actions: Exports the HTML code for editing, viewing, approving, and declining the 

transaction. 

• 
Add approver: Click to add another approver for the transaction. 

• 
Send back: Send the transaction back to the requester. 

• 
Status: Click to view the transaction’s approval history. 

Additional Menu Items 

• 
Select Copy to copy the data in the selected cell to the clipboard. You can paste the copied 

data into a text file or another application. 

• 
Select Copy with headers to copy the data in the selected cell and the cell’s column header 

to the clipboard. 

The Paste command is not for use in the Workbench at this time.

#### Accounts
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 36*



##### Before You Begin
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 36*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Accounts 

General Ledger accounts are where you post balance sheet, income statement, and statistical 

transactions. The list of a company’s accounts is called its Chart of Accounts. Accounts make it 

easier to keep track of transaction types. 
For multi-entity shared users, an account is a master object. Master objects can either be accessible to all entities, or exclusive to a specific entity. To make an account exclusive, you can enable the restrictions on the Configure Multi
-Entity page.

Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

The user must have limited or full Administrator privileges to work with accounts. 

Permissions 

These permissions are required to work with accounts. 

Application 
Activities/Lists Permissions 

General ledger 
Accounts 
List, View, Add, Edit, Delete 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 
To add or import new G/L accounts, you must be at the top level.

Chart of Accounts 

If your company was set up without a chart of accounts, you need to upload one. You can upload 

a chart of accounts that exists as an external data file in CSV format by selecting Company > All > 

Import Data. More information on uploading CSV files can be found in Preparing Your CSV File 

for Import (on page 420) and Importing a chart of accounts. When you upload a chart of 

accounts, you also have the option of automatically creating a basic set of account groups. 
You must be at the top level to add and import G/L accounts.

#### Accounts with Budgets
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 37*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Accounts with Budgets 

It’s possible to budget for both income statement and balance sheet accounts, although many 

companies include only income statement accounts in the budget. 

Income statement accounts 

Income statement accounts are reported as the activity for the reporting period, and can be 

described as for the period. The budget entry should be the budgeted activity for the period. For 

example, if the budget amount for an income statement account is 4 for January and 4 for 

February and 4 for March, the reported amount for the monthly period of March would be 4 and 

the amount for the first quarter would be 12. 

Balance sheet accounts 

The system reports balance sheet amounts as of a point in time, which can be described as 

cumulative balance as long as there’s a budget amount for each period. The budget entry for a 

period is the change in the balance sheet account. For example, if the ending budget account 

balance for January is 2 and the ending budget account balance for February is expected to be 6, 

the February budget amount is entered as 4. 
For balance sheet accounts, you must enter a budget amount for every period in order to keep a cumulative balance. If there’s no budget amount for a period, the system sees this as no budget, 
so there won’t be a cumulative amount. In this cas
e, the budget amount isn’t carried forward 
from the last period.

Statistical accounts 

Statistical accounts can also be included in a budget, and can be defined as for the period or 

cumulative when they are created. 

• 
If the statistical account is defined as for the period, the budget amount should be entered 

as the change for the period. For example, suppose that a headcount account has an 

opening budget balance of 243. If you expect headcount to go up by 10 in a particular 

month, you would enter 10 as the budget amount. 

• 
If the statistical account is defined as cumulative, the budget amount should be entered as 

of ending balance. For example, if the headcount has an opening budget balance of 243 

and you expect the headcount to go up by 10, you would enter 253 as the budget amount. 

---



#### Accounts with Categories
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 38*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Accounts with Categories 

With account categories, you can group accounts easily without worrying about restrictive 

account numbering. Out-of-box account groups and financial reports are built based on these 

account categories, automatically pulling in accounts tagged with the appropriate category, which 

means you don’t have to reconfigure reports and account groups every time you add an account. 

Instead, assign the right category, and the data will be pulled in at runtime. Using categories is 

the quickest way to get data into a report. 

Categories are available only to those companies that were created with a QuickStart setup 

template. 

You can see if your company was set up using a QuickStart on the Company information page. If 

the Setup template field on the Accounting tab is populated, then that is the template type that 

your company uses. If it is blank, then a template was not specified during setup and categories 

aren’t available to you. 

Out-of-box account groups included with a QuickStart template are already mapped to a 

category which are used in out-of-box financial reports and graphs. When you map an account to 

one of these categories, that account is automatically rolled up to the correct place in the financial 

reports and graphs. 

Import an Account-to-Account Category Mapping 

Categories are part of the QuickStart template, and are included in the chart of accounts import 

template.  

Use the template to import chart of account information, including the categories assigned to 

your accounts. 
You can’t create new categories.

The Category field works differently from other template fields to ensure that you select a 

category. You can select a category from the drop-down list or type a category in the field. The 

system validates the category. If you typed a category in the field and a category with that name 

doesn’t exist, the system sends an error message. 

---



##### Viewing the List of G/L Accounts
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 39*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

The system depends on a pre-defined list of categories. You can assign a category to an account, 

but you can’t change the category name in the template. If you change the name or type 

category that doesn’t exist, the import will fail. 

Account Categories List 

Download a list of account categories for your template and QuickStart type. 

Mapping Accounts to Account Categories Using CSV 

The best way to associate a large list of accounts with the existing categories is by uploading a 

CSV file. You edit your data in Excel, choose the appropriate categories for the accounts, and then 

upload the file to RealPage Accounting. 

Categories are used to create pre-defined, out-of-box reports and other elements in the system. 

To ensure that these reports work correctly, you can’t change existing categories or create new 

ones in the system. 

Instead, you can create your own account groups to group accounts however you’d like, and then 

use those account groups in reports. 

Viewing the List of G/L Accounts 

To view the list of accounts: 

1. 
Open the General ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Accounts.

##### Adding a G/L Account
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 40*

Accounts List 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Accounts`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Accounts`


Use the Accounts list to add and manage G/L accounts. From this list, you can add, edit, view, and 

delete account records. You can also export the list of accounts. 

Click an account to generate a general ledger report of the account. 
You must be at the top level to add and import G/L accounts.

Adding a G/L Account 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > 
 Accounts`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > 
 Accounts`


To add a G/L account: 

1. 
From the top level, open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
In the Search & Find section, click the 
 Add button next to the Accounts menu option. 

The General Ledger accounts page opens. 

3. 
Enter the account number. After you save the account record, the account number cannot 

be edited. 
We recommend using dimensions instead of sub-accounts. This simplifies reporting when 
rolling up for reports. 

4. 
In the Title field, type a name for the account as you want it to appear as the heading on 

reports, for example, Assets.  
After you save the account record, you have the option to create additional titles for the account. This option is useful when your system has multiple entities that would benefit 

from custom titles, such as account titles in other languages or a variation of an account title that an entity
’s users are more familiar with.

5. 
In the Category drop-down list, select the category of account that you are creating. 

Categories are permanently pre-assigned to account groups, which are used in financial 

reports and graphs that are supplied with the system. 

For example, you would assign the Petty Cash account to the category Cash and Cash 

Equivalents, which is assigned to the Cash and Cash Equivalents account group and appears 

on financial reports and graphs that contain this account group. If you do not want this 

account to be in your financials, then delete the text in this field so it is blank. 

---


<!-- Page 41 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

The Category field is displayed only for companies that were created with a setup template 

or chose one later. If you need to assign categories to many accounts, the easiest way is to 

export your chart of accounts, make the changes in the file, and then upload the file. 

When Your System Was Set Up With the Services Template
By default, the categories Manpowerand 
Direct materialsare assigned to account groups 
that are only used in the reports Project Profitabilityand Project Estimation Versus Actual
. These two categories do not appear in balance sheet reports or income statements. If you assign an account to either of these two categories, and you want it to show up in your balance sheet, income statement, or other reports, you must manually assign these categories to the appropriate account groups.

6. 
In the Account type drop-down list, select either Balance sheet account or Income 

statement account. 

• 
Balance sheet accounts are snapshots of the current state of a company’s assets, 

liabilities, and equity at a specific time. 

• 
Income statement accounts (sometimes called profit and loss statements) are 

cumulative for the selected period. 

7. 
For Normal balance, select either Debit or Credit. 

When you create an account, you specify whether the normal balance, from an accounting 

standpoint, is a debit or credit. For example, expense accounts are normally debit accounts. 

Sales accounts are normally a credit. If you were to run a Trial Balance report, the normal 

balance of the accounts is what determines whether the account is a credit or debit. 

8. 
For the Period end closing type, select whether the account is a closing or non-closing 

account. For closing accounts, select an account in the Close into account drop-down list. 

• 
Instead of manually closing accounts at year-end, you can set them to be closing 

accounts and then specify the account in which to close that period. 

• 
Income statement accounts should be closing accounts; balance sheet accounts 

should be non-closing accounts. 

• 
For all income statement accounts, the close-into account will typically be Retained 

Earnings. The system then zeros closing-type accounts into retained earnings at 

year-end. 

9. 
In the G/L account alternative drop-down list, select a G/L account for the system to use as 

an alternative payables account. The account override must be enabled for this field to 

appear. 

10. 
The default status is Active. If you want to prevent this G/L account from appearing in lists, 

select Inactive in the Status drop-down list. (You can still list inactive accounts by clicking 

the Include inactive check box in lists.) 

---


<!-- Page 42 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

11. 
Select the Disallow Direct Posting check box to prevent direct entry of journal entries to the 

subledger control account (for example, Accounts Payable, Accounts Receivable, etc.). 

Use this control to ensure that the account balance for the subledger account is accurate 

and has the necessary subledger details supporting the figure, rather than a direct entry that 

would not be reflected in the subledger application area. 

12. 
Select the Require Unit check box to require the user to select a unit when using this 

account. 

13. 
Select the Require asset category/type during invoice entry check box if you want to 

require users to enter asset information whenever they select this G/L account when 

entering an A/P invoice. 
This option appears when the Enable asset in invoice entrycheck box is selected in 
Accounts Payable settings.

14. 
Select the Use for budgeting check box if you want to use this G/L account in budgets. 

15. 
Select the Allow as Elimination Account check box to allow this account to be selected 

when creating an elimination entity. 

Requiring Dimensions 

Dimensions drive visibility into the parts of your business that matter most. You can require users 

to select dimensions when selecting the G/L account. When you require a dimension for a G/L 

account, all transactions that post to that account must include the dimension or dimensions that 

you select here. If you require multiple dimensions, all dimensions must be included to post to the 

G/L account. 

Use the Require Dimensions section to select the dimensions that you want to require for this 

account. 

• 
To require dimensions for transaction entries assigned to this account, select the dimension 

check boxes to require. All selected dimensions will be required. Supported dimensions 

include: 

• 
Department 

• 
Location 

• 
Customer 

• 
Vendor 

• 
Employee 

---


<!-- Page 43 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

If This Account Overrides Other Accounts 

To flag this account as an override of the default A/P or A/R G/L account, select the type of G/L 

account that it can override. Although you can flag any account as an available override account, 

typically, you flag an A/R or A/P account. 

• 
Payables account: Adds the account to the filtered list of A/P G/L accounts available as 

override accounts for bill and adjustment transactions, and for transactions involving a 

specific vendor. 

• 
Receivables account: Adds the account to the filtered list of A/R G/L accounts available as 

override accounts for invoice and adjustment transactions, and for transactions involving a 

specific customer. 

As you enter transactions, you can select the same alternative G/L account for all line items, or 

select alternative accounts individually per line item. On the Vendor and Customer pages, you 

can select one alternative G/L account to use for balancing all transactions involving that vendor. 
Currently, this feature applies to A/P invoices, A/R invoices, and adjustments only. It doesn’ t 
apply to recurring A/P invoices or A/R invoices, manual payments, manual deposits, quick check entry, quick A/P invoice entry, quick A/R invoice entry, or advances.

Including the Account in Chart Types 

You can simplify the accounting task by creating subsets of the chart of accounts. These subsets 

are called chart types. The Chart types option appears when your company has the chart type 

feature enabled. 

If your company uses chart types, every time you create a new account, consider adding the 

account to each appropriate chart type. 

To add an account to a chart type: 

1. 
Click the Chart types’ Select button. The multi-select box opens. 

2. 
Select the chart type in which to include this account. 
You can select more than one chart type if you need to add the account to multiple chart types.

Filling In the Reporting Accounts Mapping Section 

• 
If you set up reporting accounts that represent alternate charts of accounts, you can use the 

Reporting accounts mapping section to map G/L accounts (on page 34). 

• 
You can also generate and display reports using account sets other than your General 

Ledger accounts. This report is typically used by companies that have clients or other 

governing bodies who need to see reports in their charts of accounts. See the Reporting 

Accounts Mapping report in online help or download the RealPage General Ledger Reports

Filling In the Tax Code Fields 

The Tax return code and M-3 return code fields display only if you have enabled tax codes on 

the General Ledger setup page. Use these fields if you use third-party software applications to 


> **Navigation Path:** `prepare client tax returns. 
To enable tax codes for your G/Laccounts, point to Company > Setup > Subscriptions. On the 
Subscriptions page, click
 General Ledger, and then click 
Configure. When the 
General ledger`


setuppage appears, find the 
GAAP and taxsection, and select
 Enable tax codes.

Enter the tax code in the Tax return code field to map tax codes to the tax forms you need to use. 

(Whether you fill out one or both of these tax code boxes depends on which item or items your 

tax software requires.) 

For example, if you use Thomson Reuters GoSystem Tax, and you want to map tax return codes to 

a tax form (such as Form 1120, Form 1065, etc.), you must enter the tax code in the Tax return 

code field. Similarly, to map M-3 return codes to your M-3 form, you must enter the tax code in 

the M-3 return code field. 
You can also input tax codes for all of your G/Laccounts as a group and then import the spreadsheet that will map all of the required data from the system to its corresponding places on your tax forms.To do this: Open the Company menu,
 and then click 
Import Data.

Set Up Company Master List section, next to the listing for Account Tax Return Codes, click

Mapping Reporting Accounts to G/L Accounts 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Search & Find >

The Map G/L Accounts to Reporting Accounts section appears after you save a new reporting 

account set. This mapping is what enables the system to display a report using a different 

reporting account set.  

Using the options in the mapping section, you can: 

• 
Map from General Ledger accounts to reporting accounts in the set. 

• 
Map to existing reporting accounts, or create new ones on the fly. 

• 
Filter your mappings based on different criteria, such as looking for unmapped accounts or 

accounts that are private to an entity. 

To add or edit a reporting account set: 

1. 
In the General Ledger application, select the All tab. 

2. 
In the Search & Find section, click Reporting accounts. 

3. 
Click the Edit link. 

Mapping a Reporting Account to a G/L Account 

You can map a single reporting account to one or more General Ledger accounts. All reporting 

accounts that you map must be the same type (balance sheet, income statement, or statistical 

accounts). In addition, the G/L account and the reporting accounts must be the same type. 

To map a reporting account to a G/L account:  

1. 
Select the check box of the G/L Account that you want to map. 

2. 
Click the Reporting account column, and then open the drop-down list. Select the desired 

reporting account to map to the G/L account. 
You can create reporting accounts on the fly by selecting the Add link in the drop
- down 
list, and then creating a new reporting account. You can also change a reporting account by selecting the reporting account that you want to change and then clicking the View link in 
the drop-down list. The reporting account page opens. Click the 
Edit button to make 
changes. 

3. 
Click Save to save the mapping. The pages refresh to show that the Mapped check box is 

now selected. 

Mapping a Reporting Account to Multiple G/L Accounts 

To map multiple G/L accounts to one reporting account: 

1. 
Select all G/L accounts that you want to map to one reporting account.

##### Inactivating or Deleting an Account
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 48*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Inactivating or Deleting an Account 

You can inactivate an account you no longer want to prevent yourself and users from seeing it in 

lists. You can also delete an account from the Accounts list, unless the account is used in a book 

transaction. After you delete an account, it cannot be restored. If you’re unsure about deleting an 

account, set its status to Inactive. 
If you want to be warned when you’re about to delete an account or other type of record f
rom the system, click your login name in the upper-left corner of any page, and then click 
My preferences. 
On the Preferences page, select the 
Warn on delete check box.

You can make a G/L account inactive only if it is not associated with any open online purchase 

orders or eInvoices. 

To inactivate an account: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Click the Accounts option to open the list of your accounts. 

3. 
Find the account that you want to inactivate, and then click the Edit link. 

4. 
On the General Ledger Accounts page, change the Status of the account to “Inactive,” and 

then click Save. 
If the G/L account that you are trying to inactivate is associated with a transaction, then the system displays an error message warning you that you cannot delete a G/L account that is associated with an open online purchase order or eInvoice. This warning ensures that all orders and electronic invoices successfully arrive in RealPage Accounting.

To delete an account: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Click the Accounts option to open the list of your accounts. 

3. 
Find the account you want to remove, and then click the Delete link. 

4. 
Click OK to confirm the deletion. 

If a G/L account is referenced by an account label, you must remove the account label 

before you can inactivate or delete the account. Account labels are set up in A/P and A/R. 

---

Viewing and Printing Account Information 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Accounts`


You can view a list of accounts from the Accounts list, or you can view and print the details of a 

particular account from the General Ledger Account page. 

The items you can see on this page vary slightly depending on whether your company is an 

Enterprise, or a multi-entity shared company.  

If your company is a multi-entity shared company, when viewing the list of G/L accounts at the 

top level, you initially see all items regardless of where they were created. To see items created 

only at the top level, clear the Include private check box.  

G/L account records created prior to 2021 could be created at the entity level. G/L account 

records that you create today can be created only at the top level.  

• 
To include the older private records in the list, select the Include private check box. The 

Entity column appears in the list and shows the entity to which the (vendor) record belongs.  

• 
To exclude private records, clear the Include private check box. 

Also, when you edit an item that was created in an entity, your changes apply to that entity. After 

you save your changes, you are returned to the top level. 

To view accounts: 

1. 
Open the General ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Accounts. The list of accounts opens showing your chart of accounts.  

3. 
Find the account you want to view in more detail, and then click the View link. 

To print account information: 

1. 
From the Accounts list, find the account you want to print, and then click the View link. 

2. 
On the General Ledger Accounts page, click Print to, and then select either PDF or XML for 

your printing format.

Specifying Different Default Accounts by Property 

You can select default accounts at the entity level that are different from those specified at the top 

level. 

To use this feature: 

• 
The chart types feature must be enabled. 

• 
You must have administrator-level privileges. 

• 
Your system must be a multi-entity shared environment. 

• 
To define a default Accounts payable G/L account and an Undeposited funds account in 

Accounts Payable, the Management Fees feature must be enabled. 

• 
To define a default Accounts receivable G/L account and an Undeposited funds account in 

Accounts Receivable, the Intercompany Billing feature must be enabled. 

Defining a Default Accounts Payable G/L Account 

To enable a default A/P account for a property: 

1. 
Open the property’s entity. 

2. 
Open the Accounts Payable menu, and then click the Setup tab. 

3. 
Select Configuration. 

4. 
In the Account settings section, find the G/L accounts section. 

5. 
In the Accounts payable drop-down list, select the desired G/L account to make it the 

property’s default A/P account. 

6. 
Click Save. 

Defining a Default Accounts Receivable G/L Account 

 To enable a default A/R account for a property: 

1. 
Open the property’s entity. 

2. 
Open the Accounts Receivable menu, and then click the Setup tab. 

3. 
Select Configuration. 

4. 
In the Account settings section, find the G/L accounts section. 

5. 
In the Accounts receivable drop-down list, select the desired G/L account to make it the 

property’s default A/R account.

Click Save.

Fields 

Accounts List Columns 

Account number 
This is the unique number of the account. This number can’t be 

edited after you save the account record.

This is the name of the account. 
Be sure to include the purpose of the account in the Title field.

Normal balance 
Indicates whether the normal balance of this account is debit or 

credit. 

Req department 
Informs you if transactions assigned to this account must have a 

department attached to complete the transaction entry. 

Req location 
Informs you if transactions assigned to this account must have a 

location attached to complete the transaction entry. 

Closing type 
Accounts can be Closing or Non-closing. Income statement 

accounts should be closing accounts; balance sheet accounts 

should be non-closing accounts. This feature takes the ending 

balance for each reporting period and rolls it into the Closed to 

account (which is typically Retained earnings), effectively making 

the balance forward for the G/L account zero. This is especially 

important for income statement accounts to eliminate the need 

for adjustment entries into the G/L journal at the end of each 

reporting period. 

Closes into 
This is the Closed to account mentioned in Closing type, which will 

typically be Retained earnings. 

Disallow direct posting 
This column indicates whether the account allows direct postings 

or not.

fly or, in many cases, by having the system automatically add them 

from a preset numbering scheme.

#### Account Groups

Account Groups 

Account groups help you organize your accounts into larger and more manageable groups for 

financial reports, graphs, and performance cards. If you are new to account groups, we 

recommend that you start by reviewing the Account Groups overview (on page 45). 

---



#### Account Groups Overview
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 55*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Account Groups Overview 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`



> **Navigation Path:** `Application > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Account Groups`


Account groups are the fundamental building blocks of financial reporting in RealPage 

Accounting. 

Account groups simplify reporting by organizing accounts into reusable structures (such as 

“Assets” and “Expenses”) that are separate from the reports, graphs, and performance cards that 

use them. These structures: 

• 
Roll up and organize accounts. 

• 
Create the headings and subtotals in reports. 
General Ledger accounts are shown on financial statements only if they are included in an account group. Accounts can’t be added directly to a repor
t.

Although every company’s account groups will be different, your account groups will likely 

contain groupings related to assets, expenses, revenue, and more. 

The most basic types of account groups contain either individual accounts or other account 

groups. 

• 
Account groups of type Accounts, Statistical accounts, Category, or Statistical category are 

used to select individual accounts. 

For example, “Cash and Cash Equivalents” might contain some bank accounts and petty 

cash. 

• 
An account group of type Group of account groups contains other account groups. 

For example, “Assets” might contain other account groups, such as “Current Assets” and 

“Fixed Assets,” which in turn contain other account groups. 

---


<!-- Page 56 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

By nesting groups, you can create a hierarchy of groupings for reporting purposes, with the 

lowest level containing individual accounts. 

a) Account groups can contain other groups. Nesting groups creates hierarchy. 

b) At the lowest levels, account groups contain individual accounts. 
You cannot use computation account groups in financial forecasting reports. Because in forecasting reports you could use an “As of date
”in a partial month, mixing part actual numbers 
and part budget, the computation needed cannot accommodate computation account groups.

---



##### Reusable Building Blocks
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 57*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Reusable Building Blocks 

Since account groups are maintained separately from the financial statements that use them, they 

are easy to manage and update. 

• 
Account groups (such as Assets) can be used in multiple reports. 

• 
As you add or modify accounts, you need only make sure that they’re in the right account 

groups. 

• 
Changes to the account group affect all reports that use the account group. 

---



##### Role in Financial Reports, Graphs, and Performance Cards
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 58*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Role in Financial Reports, Graphs, and Performance Cards 

Account groups can be used across any number of reports, graphs, and dashboard performance 

cards. 

When you create a financial report or graph, you are asked to select the account group or groups 

that you want to include. In the example below, the rows in a balance sheet report are formed by 

adding two account groups: Assets, and Liabilities and Equity. 

---


<!-- Page 59 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

When these top-level account groups are included in a report, the system automatically includes 

all subgroups and accounts. In the report, each account group gets a heading row and a total 

row, with indentation used to depict the hierarchy. Additionally, each top-level account group 

(such as Assets and Liabilities and Equity) receives a grand total. 

Account groups let you organize accounts and facilitate headings and totals on reports. 

When you design your account groups, keep in mind where you want headings and totals to 

appear, and where you want your grand totals. In income statements, it’s typical to have only one 

account group (Net income), which contains other account groups. That way, your income 

statements contain a grand total for Net income. 
By default, account groups are fully expanded on reports to show the complete hierarchy. However, you can create reports using the same account groups, but with different levels of detail.

---



##### Account Groups and the Calculation Method
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 60*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Performance cards, often included at the top of dashboards, are also based on account groups. 

Each performance card is based on a single account group, which determines the value that users 

see. Although you can use any account group as the basis for a performance card, it’s common to 

use a computation account group, which consist of a mathematical equation. For example, Net 

Profit Margin can be constructed as Net Income divided by Total Income. 

Account Groups and the Calculation Method 

When using account groups in reports, you’ll want the report to calculate only the amounts in the 

period you select. Every account group has a default method of calculating amounts on reports. 

For example, account groups that contain income or expense accounts are typically calculated for 

the period. Calculation methods can be: 

• 
For period: calculate only the amounts in the period you selected 

• 
Start of period: calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the start of the selected period 

• 
End of period: calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the end of the selected period 

The latter is useful in a balance-forward situation. For example, say your fiscal year runs from 

January 1 to December 31. If you run a report in March, choosing Start of period totals the data 

from January 1 to March 1. 

The Interest expensecategory is configured to Credit normal, even though sometimes it
’ s 
assumed to be Debit normal. This is intentional. The interest expense account has a debit balance as the default, but in reporting it appears as a negative amount, offsetting the interest income.

---



##### Organize Accounts and Create Hierarchy
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 61*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Organize Accounts and Create Hierarchy 

You’ll notice that your chart of accounts is flat, without hierarchy. For example, individual expense 

accounts aren’t rolled up into Expenses or Operating Expenses. Instead, the organization of 

accounts for reporting is done outside of the chart of accounts, using account groups. 

For example, your chart of accounts might include: 

• 
Revenue - Services 

• 
Revenue - Subscriptions 

• 
Expenses - Marketing 

• 
Expenses - Payroll 

• 
Expenses - Utilities 

These accounts aren’t included directly in financial reports, such as a Net Income report. Instead, 

financial reports are built from account groups, which organize accounts and determine where 

subtotals and totals are created. 
The same account can be part of multiple account groups, and you can create a hierarchy of 
account groups.

Groups of Accounts 

The simplest account group is one level deep, and consists of member accounts. When you are 

establishing an account group hierarchy, start by creating groups of member accounts. 

• 
To create a group of G/L accounts, select Accounts as the structure type. 

• 
To create a group of statistical accounts, select Statistical accounts as the structure type. 

---


<!-- Page 62 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

The following example shows a “Cash and Equivalents” account group, which contains a range of 

G/L accounts. 

a) The system uses the name for headings and total rows on reports. 

b) The Structure type is Accounts. 

c) This is the range of accounts in this group. 

In the example above, notice that the structure type is Accounts, which means that it contains 

individual accounts or ranges of accounts. 

If your company was configured using a setup template, you already have a set of pre-defined 

account groups based on the Category or Statistical category. These work the same way as 

groups of accounts or statistical accounts, except that they are based on the category assigned to 

accounts rather than a specific list of accounts. 

Groups of Groups 

After you’ve created a group of member accounts, you can build the next level of the hierarchy, 

which consists of groups that contain other groups. For example, you might create a Current 

Assets account group that contains other account groups. 

---


<!-- Page 63 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

a) The Structure type is Group of account groups. 

b) Select the groups of account groups to include. 

---



##### Types of Account Groups
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 64*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Types of Account Groups 

These are the simplest and most basic type of account groups. They consist of one or more G/L 

accounts from your chart of accounts. You can include individual named accounts or ranges of 

accounts. 

For example, you might create a Cash and Cash Equivalents account group that contains various 

bank and investment accounts. 

Account groups 

A group of account groups is one that contains other account groups, thus forming a hierarchy. 

You can have groups within groups within groups, and so on, to any level of depth. 

---


<!-- Page 65 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

For example, the Assets account group might consist of four discrete account groups—Current 

Assets, Fixed Assets, Intangible Assets, and Other Assets—each of which contains additional 

subgroups. 

In the graphic above, the account groups are collapsed to show only a summary amount. When 

you view a report online, you can use the expand/collapse arrows to control the level of detail. 

Statistical Accounts 

Statistical account groups include accounts that contain specific non-financial data – for example, 

headcount, hospital beds, square footage, or hotel rooms. Using statistical account groups, you 

can calculate against and report on data to get a virtually unlimited variety of ratios and business 

metrics, such as average deal size or revenue per hotel room. 

---


<!-- Page 66 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Here’s how the occupancy statistical account group, with some of its constituent statistical 

accounts, would display in a report: 

Category or Statistical Category 

These apply if you used a setup template when you first created your company in RealPage 

Accounting. If so, the system automatically assigns pre-configured account groups to each of the 

accounts in your chart of accounts; those account groups, in turn, are used in pre-designed 

standard financial reports and graphs. Creating new categories is not supported. 

As you create new accounts, you tag each new account with a category, which automatically 

enrolls that account into the relevant account groups. 

A group that is based on a category displays the same way as a group of accounts. The only 

difference is that the accounts for the group are selected by category. 

---



##### Account Group Examples
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 67*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Computation 

Computation account groups consist of other account groups or individual accounts that you use 

as components in a mathematical equation. The results of the equation display in your financial 

report.  

For example, Revenue Per Employee could be constructed as Revenue GL account / Employees 

statistical account. Similarly, you could construct an account group as Revenue. 

Computation account group is the only type of account group that allows you to set the number 

of decimal places. Any computation account group includes this option. 

• 
A commonly used computation-type account group shows an operating margin using the 

formula Operating net income divided by Net sales. Define the number of decimal places 

and type, and optionally include the unit used, such as number or percent. 

• 
To use more than the default two decimal places without affecting the actual numbers, 

create a computation account group that: 

• 
Includes only one account group. 

• 
Has a computation formula as Constant, and that constant is set to one. 

• 
Has an action to multiply the selected account group. 

• 
Has Decimal places set to the number of decimal places to display when no rounding 

is applied. The minimum is zero and the maximum is nine. 

You can then use that group as part of other groups, as a standalone group, or in performance 

cards on dashboards. 

Account Group Examples 

Here are some examples of account groups that you might find useful. 

• 
An account group called “Withdrawal” that’s filtered for credit only on your bank accounts; 

the system will display only credit transactions such as withdrawal transactions in the report. 

• 
An account group that contains summary revenues for a single location, such as Sales - 

Location 1, and include it as a row in a report. 

• 
An account group that contains only the debit transactions to an equipment account, such 

as Equipment Purchases. 

---



##### Account Groups and Normal Balance
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 68*



##### Organize Through Account Groups
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 68*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Account Groups and Normal Balance 

The normal balance of the account group adjusts whether the accounts in the group are a credit 

or debit, in the context of a financial report. 

• 
If the Normal balance of an account group is Credit, then credit accounts appear as a 

positive and debit accounts appear as a negative. 

• 
If the Normal balance of an account group is Debit, then credit accounts appear as a 

negative and debit accounts appear as a positive. 

For example, Expenses is a debit account group and the accounts in the group, such as Utilities, 

are typically debit accounts. This causes the accounts in Expenses to appear as a positive number 

on income statements. Additionally, Net Income is a credit account group, which causes Expenses 

to be treated as a debit when determining total net income. 

Organize Through Account Groups 

You’ll also notice that your chart of accounts is flat, without hierarchy. For example, individual 

expense accounts aren’t rolled up into Expenses or Operating Expenses. Instead, the organization 

of accounts for reporting is done outside of the chart of accounts, using account groups. 

For example, your chart of accounts might include: 

• 
Revenue - Services 

• 
Revenue - Subscriptions 

• 
Expenses - Marketing 

• 
Expenses - Payroll 

• 
Expenses - Utilities 

These accounts aren’t included directly in financial reports, such as a Net Income report. Instead, 

financial reports are built from account groups, which organize accounts and determine where 

subtotals and totals are created. 

The same account can be part of multiple account groups, and you can create a hierarchy of 

account groups. 

Example 

The following graphic illustrates one way that accounts can work together with account groups 
and dimensions in a financial report. 

In this sample report: 

• 
The highlighted accounts come from the chart of accounts. 

• 
These accounts are organized into account groups (a), which create the groupings and 
totals on report rows. 

• 
Columns are set up to compare by the location dimension (b). 

---

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

a) Account groups: Organize your accounts and create headings and totals. 

b) Dimensions: Lets you divide your data for more granular reporting.

##### Multi-Entity Report Structures
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 70*

Search & Find

Multi-Entity Report Structures 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > Subscriptions`


Multi-Entity Shared > Multi-Entity Report Structures 

You can create report structures both at the top level and at the entity level of a multi-entity 

shared company. 

In practice, many companies create and manage the majority of account groups and dimension 

structures at the top level of the company. These report structures are then automatically shared 

by all entities, and available for use in financial reports, graphs, and performance cards. 

Best Practices 

To determine whether to create a report structure at the top level or entity level, consider who 

needs to access the structure. 

• 
If a report structure should be available to all entities, create it at the top level. Account 

groups and dimension structures created at the top level automatically appear in the 

corresponding lists in each entity. 
Report structures created at the top level can be edited only at the top level.

• 
If an entity has particular reporting needs, users can create report structures at the entity 

level. These report structures can then be used within that entity, along with any report 

structures that were inherited from the top level. 
When a user switches to an entity, the user can’t see report structures that were created 
in another entity.

Top-Level Report Structures 

From the top level of a multi-entity shared company, you can edit and use all reporting structures, 

including those created at the entity level. 

• 
By default, lists initially display only report structures that were created at the top level. 

• 
Click the Include private check box at the top of the list to see report structures that were 

created in an entity. You can edit and use these report structures provided that you have 

access to the entities in which they were created. 

• 
You can Add new top-level report structures and Edit existing structures that were created 

at either the top level or entity level. 

Include Private Does Not Appear on Your Page 

The most common reason that you don’t see this feature is because you’re using a custom view. 

When you select a custom view, some options are hidden. Depending on the page you’re on, the 

Include inactive, Include one-time, Include private, and Display hierarchy options aren’t visible. 

---

For example, if you’re viewing from a custom view, the Financial reports list won’t provide the 

Include inactive option. 

To see the options again, switch back to View all. See Custom Views for more information. 

Entity-Level Report Structures 

When you switch to an entity, you can use report structures that were created at the top level, 

plus any report structures that were created in that entity. 

• 
You can add new report structures and edit existing structures that were created at the 

entity level. 

• 
View (but not edit) report structures that were inherited from the top level. You’ll recognize 

these by the Not owned status. This means that the entity can use these report structures, 

but they can be edited only at the top level. 

Importing Account Groups 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`


You can import and export account group data and group member data. Each type of data has its 

own template file. Note that the account group template is different from account group member 

template. 

This procedure describes importing account group data. You must import account groups first 

and then import account group members second, because the account groups must exist before 

you can import the account group members (on page 63). 

If this is the first time that you are creating account groups, then follow the instructions in First 

Time Account Group Imports. Otherwise, follow the instruction in Importing Account Groups to 

Update the System. 

First Time Account Group Imports 

1. 
Open the Company menu. 

2. 
Click Import data. 

3. 
Find the Set Up Company Master List section, and then find the Account groups row. 

4. 
Click the Default template link. The system downloads the file to your browser’s default 

location.  

5. 
Go to Step 2, Preparing the Data. 

Importing Account Groups to Update the System 

Step 1: Exporting from the Account Groups List  

You can export account group information from the Account Groups list.  


> **Navigation Path:** `1. 
Go to the Account Groups list (General Ledger > All > Search & Find > Account groups).`


---


<!-- Page 72 -->

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

2. 
Click the Export button. A box appears with two choices, Account Groups or Member 

Groups.  

3. 
Select Account Groups. 

Step 2: Preparing the Data 

When you download a template from the Import data list, the correct headers are in the template 

already. Fill in the columns with your data, save it as a CSV file, and then upload the file. The 

header titles in the template that you download correlate to fields in the system. Incorrect header 

titles will cause an upload error.  

For more information about the CSV upload process, including best practices, common errors, 

and how to upload your CSV file, see Preparing Your CSV File for Import (on page 420).  

1. 
Open the file with Microsoft Excel. 

2. 
Add information about account groups to the template. 

3. 
Fill in the required columns in the template: 

• 
GROUP_NAME: Type a name for the account group that is unique. This name is case 

sensitive. 

• 
NORMAL_BALANCE: Type DB for debit, CR for credit. 

• 
MEMBER_TYPE: Type G for Groups, A for G/L accounts, S for statistical accounts, 

COMP for Computation, C for Category, and SC for Stat Category. 

• 
F_DEPARTMENT: Type the department. This field is required when 

F_DEPARTMENT_TYPE is set to H or T. 

• 
F_LOCATION: Type the location. This field is required when F_LOCATION_TYPE is set to 

H or T. 

4. 
Optionally, add data into the remaining columns.  

5. 
Save the file as a CSV file type. If you are saving from Excel, choose the comma-delimited 

file type.  

The file will have the file extension .csv after you save it. Leave the file open until your 

import is successful. Excel can sometimes reformat the file if you close it before the import is 

successful.  

Step 3: Importing the Account Group Data 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Click Account groups. 

3. 
Click the Import button.  

If the link says Default Import, click the link to have the system automatically create a list of 

default account groups.

Importing Account Group Members 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Search & Find > Account Groups > Import > 
Group Members`


You can import and export account group members. Before you import group members, you 

must import account groups first, because the groups must exist before you can update the 

members. See Importing account groups (on page 61). 

Downloading the Template 

1. 
Open the Company menu. 

2. 
Click Import Data. 

3. 
Find the Set Up Company Master List section, and then find the Group Members row. 

4. 
Click the Template link. The system downloads the file to your browser’s default location. In 

this CSV file, each account group is mapped to a list of group members.  

Preparing the Data 

When you download a template from the Import data list, the correct headers are in the template 

already. Fill in the columns with your data, and then save it as a CSV file. The header titles in the 

template that you download correlate to fields in the system. Incorrect header titles will cause an 

upload error.  

For more information about the CSV upload process, including best practices, common errors, 

and how to upload your CSV file, see Preparing your CSV file for import (on page 420).  

1. 
Open the file with Microsoft Excel. 

2. 
Add information about an account group’s members to the template. 

3. 
Fill in the required columns in the template: 

• 
GROUP_NAME: Type a name for the account group member that is unique. This name 

is case sensitive. 

• 
CALC_AMOUNT: Type the code for the calculated amount: type F for For Period, S for 

Start of Period, and E for End of Period. 

• 
APPLY_BUDGET: This column fills the Apply calculation to budget field. Type T for 

true, F for false. This column is required when MEMBER_TYPE is set to A or S. 

• 
LEFT_OPERAND_CALC_AMT: Type F for For Period, S for Start of Period, or E for End of 

Period. This column is required when the MEMBER_TYPE is set to COMP and 

LEFT_OPERAND_ACCOUNT is not blank. 

• 
RIGHT_OPERAND_CALC_AMT: Type F for For Period, S for Start of Period, or E for End 

of Period. This column is required when the MEMBER_TYPE is set to COMP, and 

RIGHT_OPERAND_ACCOUNT is not blank.

#### Account Groups List

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide

Account Groups List 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account groups`


The Account groups list has two views, which you can switch between by using the Flip view 

button: 

• 
Account groups: Use this view to review and edit your account groups.  

• 
Accounts to account group map: Use this view to see if any accounts have not been 

assigned or have been assigned twice. 
Financial reports, graphs, and performance cards consist only of account groups – not individual 
accounts. For this reason, every account should be a member of an account group.

When you first display the Account groups list, the default view shows your existing account 

groups. From this list, you can click the Add button to create a new account group. 

For each account group, the list displays its name, the Display as title used on reports and graphs, 

and the type of account group. Other links allow you to get more information about the hierarchy 

of the account group and how it is used in reports and graphs. 
For multi-entity shared users, account groups can either be co
mmon (accessible) to all entities, or exclusive (private) to a specific entity.

Options vary for account groups in a multi-entity shared environment. For example, the top level 

includes an Include Private check box for displaying entity-level account groups. At the entity 

level, account groups created at the top level are listed as Not owned and can’t be edited. Learn 

more about account groups in a multi-entity shared environment. 

When the Include Private Check Box Does Not Appear 

The most common reason that you don’t see this feature is because you’re using a custom view. 

When you select a custom view, some options are hidden. Depending on the page you’re on, the 

Include Inactive, Include One-Time, Include Private, and Display Hierarchy check box options 

aren’t visible. For example, if you’re viewing from a custom view, the Financial reports list won’t 

provide the Include Inactive option. 

To see the options again, switch back to View all. See Custom list views for more information.

##### Account Groups Workflow
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 77*
