Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

Permissions 

These permissions are required to add and manage account groups. 

Application 
Activities/Lists 
Permissions 

General Ledger 
 Account groups 
Add, Delete, Edit, List, View 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 
You must be at the top level to add and import G/L accounts.

Account Groups Workflow 

It is typical to begin by creating account groups that will consist only of individual G/L accounts or 

discrete categories. Then create the other types of account groups (those that contain other 

account groups, those that are part of a computational formula, and so on) that are more likely to 

form the basis of your financial reports. 

The creation of an account group breaks down into four steps: 

1. 
Specify basic information about the account group. 

2. 
Define the group structure type and calculation method. 

3. 
Add members to the group. 

4. 
(Optional) Add filters to the account group. 

---



##### Adding an Account Group
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 78*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Adding an Account Group 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Search & Find > 
 Account Groups`


To add an account group: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
In the Search & Find section, click 
 Add next to Account groups. The Account group 

information page opens. 

3. 
Type a name for the new account group in the Name field. 
The name of the account group determines how it appears in lists. After saving the account group, you cannot change the name. However, you can control how the name is displayed in reports using the Display asfields.
It requires an administratorto change the name. The administrator can use the Company 
application’s Customer Utility Tool to change the account group name with the 
ID Change
menu option. 

4. 
In the Display on report as field, you can change the name that the system displays in the 

report. 

5. 
In the Display total line as field, type the name that you want the system to use for the 

heading for totals. 

6. 
In the Normal balance drop-down list, select Debit to use this account group as a debit 

balance, or select Credit to use it as a credit balance. Cash is typically a debit balance; sales 

is typically a credit balance. 

7. 
In the Account group purpose drop-down list, select the intended use for the account 

group. 

Assigning an account group purpose can help you organize your accounts into more 

manageable groups for financial reports, graphs, and performance cards. Think about how 

the account group will be used. 

8. 
In the Account group manager field, type the name of the person who should be consulted 

about changing this account group. It can be a fellow staff member or a partner who 

implemented the account group.  
If you’re using the 
Account group manageroption, consider creating a custom view for the 
list of account groups that includes the Account group manager column.

9. 
If your company uses Global Consolidations, then select the Is a KPI check box to make the 

account group available as a key performance indicator (KPI). 

---


<!-- Page 79 -->
Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

This check box is available only for multi-entity shared companies that use Global 
Consolidations.

You can view real-time KPI data for any entity or location in the Organization view when KPI 

rollup is enabled. You can have as many KPIs as you want selected in account groups, but 

only the first 10 KPIs (alphabetically) are shown in the Organization view. 
Only the first 10 account groups marked as KPIs appear in the organization view
’s KPI 
pop-up. To ensure that your desired KPIs appear in the list, name (or rename) them with a letter or number prefix, such as a_Assets, b_Cash and equivalents, c_Expenses, and so
on.

10. 
If you enable KPIs for account groups later, the Refresh button in the Organization chart 

refreshes only existing data. Refresh the page in the browser to see the new KPI data. 

Defining the Group Structure Type and Calculation Method 

1. 
In the Structure type drop-down list, select the structure type that you want to assign to the 

account group: 

• 
Accounts 

• 
Group of accounts 

• 
Statistical accounts 

• 
Computation 

• 
Category 

• 
Statistical category 

The page options change depending on which group type you select. 
Each st ructure type is designed for a specific use and has different features. You can no longer change the structure type after you save the account group. If you mistakenly assigned a different structure type and have not used the account group in any report, 
delete the group and recreate it with the correct structure type.

2. 
Select the calculation method from the Calculation method drop-down list. 

• 
For period: Calculate only the amounts in the selected period. 

• 
Start of period: Calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the start of the selected 

period. 

• 
End of period: Calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the end of the selected 

period. 

---


<!-- Page 80 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

For example, in a balance-forward situation, you can set up balance sheet account groups as 

End of period and set up income statement account groups as For period. 

Computation structures use calculation methods that enable you to create formulas for your 

account group. You can add, subtract, multiply, or divide between selected accounts or account 

groups. You can also use a constant number as part of the calculation. Display the amount result 

as a ratio, percentage, or average, based on the formula that you select. 

Calculation structures can take as few as two accounts, account groups, or constants. For example, 

you can: 

• 
Divide the revenue G/L account by the employee statistical account to obtain your revenue 

per employee. 

• 
Divide your total debt by your current assets to get the debt ratio. 

A G/L account can be a member of more than one account group. 

Next, add accounts to the account group. 

---



##### Setting Up a Computation Account Group
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 81*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Setting Up a Computation Account Group 

Computation structures use calculation methods that enable you to create formulas in your 

account group. You can add, subtract. multiply, or divide between selected accounts or account 

groups. You can also use a constant number as part of the calculation. Display the amount result 

as a ratio, percentage, or average, based on the formula you select. 

Calculation structures can only take two accounts, account groups, or constants. For example, you 

can: 

• 
Divide the revenue G/L account by the Employee statistical account to obtain your revenue 

per employee. 

• 
Divide your total debt by your current assets to get the debt ratio. 

To set up a computation formula: 

1. 
On the left side of the equation, at the top of the two boxes, specify whether you want to 

use a constant, a single G/L account, or an account group. These are discrete account 

groups you want this account group to consist of. To do this, select an account group from 

the Account group drop-down list. 

2. 
Repeat this for the right side of the equation. 

3. 
Specify whether you want to add, subtract, multiply, or divide. 

Setting the Display Options 

Use display options to define the number and unit of measurement for the computation. The 

options include decimal places, type (such as number or percentage), unit, and alignment. 

1. 
In the Decimal places drop-down list, select the number of decimal places to display. 

2. 
In the next drop-down list, select what the item is. 

---


<!-- Page 82 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

3. 
In the Unit field, type the unit of measurement, or type the text that you want to appear 

next to the item. 

4. 
Specify additional account groups until you’ve included all of the groups you want in this 

computation account group. 
Some operators (add, subtract, multiply, divide) aren’t compatible with all display options. 
For example, addition, subtraction, and multiplication can’t be displayed as a percentage.

Example 1 

To display the item as X each using only whole numbers, set: 

• 

• 
Number 

• 
Each (unit) 

• 
right 

Example 2 

To display the item as $ X, use these settings:  

• 

• 
number 

• 
$ 

• 
left

Viewing and Editing Account Groups 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`


To view and edit account groups: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
In the Search & Find section, click the Account Groups option. 

3. 
Find the account group that you want to view or edit. 

4. 
Click the Edit or View link next to the desired account group. 
You can’t edit the name of the account group, but you can use the 
Display asfields to 
change howit appears in reports.

5. 
Make changes as needed. You can: 

• 
Change the calculation method. 

• 
Add more accounts (on page 73) to the account group. 

• 
Remove an account from the account group. 

• 
Change the account group filters (on page 89). 

6. 
When you have made the desired changes, you can view the hierarchy report, or choose a 

financial report or graph that uses the account group as a way to test your changes. 

• 
Click the Hierarchy link to view the Account Groups Hierarchy report. 

• 
Click the Reports link, and then click the HTML link of the desired financial report to 

review the report that uses the account group that you changed.  

• 
Click the Graphs link, and then click the View graph link to view the desired graph. 

7. 
Click Save. 

If an account is missing from an account group, or is assigned to the wrong group, you can fix it. 

See Adding an Account to an Account Group (on page 73). You can also remove an account from 

an account group.

Deleting an Account Group 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account groups`


Account groups can be deleted as long as they aren’t used by any report or object such as: 

• 
Financial report 

• 
Chart 

• 
Performance card 

• 
Project summary statistic 

To delete an account group: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Account groups. 

3. 
Find the account group, and then click the Delete link. 

4. 
Click OK to confirm that you want to delete this account group. 

If you try to delete an account group that’s used by a report object, clicking OK displays a 

message telling you that the account group is being used, and then shows the report object 

that is using it. 

Change the report object to use a different account group or delete the object before 

deleting the account group. 

Exporting from the Account Groups List 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Search & Find > Account Groups > Export`


You can export account group information from the Account groups list. 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Account groups. 

3. 
Click the Export button. 

4. 
Click either Account Groups or Member Groups, depending on whether you want to export 

data about account groups or about the members of each account group, respectively. 

The list exports the selected data as a CSV file.

Account-to-Account Group Map List 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups > Flipview`


Use the Accounts to acct group map page to see if any accounts have not been assigned or have 

been assigned twice. 

In the Account to acct group map view, your chart of accounts is directly associated with account 

names and groups. This correlation makes it easy for you to determine not only which group the 

account is assigned to, but also whether any accounts have not been assigned or have been 

assigned twice. You make the initial correlation using the Account groups view, and then use the 

Account to acct group map view to check your work.  

To make edits after you have finished checking your list in the Account to acct group map view, 

click the Flipview button again to return to the Account groups view, and then click the Members 

link.

Account Group Information Page 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups > Add/Edit`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Account Groups > Add/Edit`


Use the Account group information page to create and modify account groups that you use for 

reporting purposes. You can also duplicate the account group and view the account group 

record’s audit trail from this page. 

Account groups are the fundamental building blocks of financial reports. Every account that you 

have must be part of an account group if you want to use its data in a balance sheet, income 

statement, cash flow, or other report. If you are new to account groups, we recommend that you 

review the Account groups overview (on page 45). 

This page has three sections: Summary, Middle, and Report Filters. 

• 
Summary: The summary section at the top of the page lets you set up how the account 

group’s name and total appear in a report. You also set the normal balance and select the 

manager and purpose of the account group. You can also designate the account group as a 

key performance indicator (KPI). 

• 
Middle: Use the middle section to select the calculation method and add accounts to the 

account group.  

• 
Report filters: Use this section to filter the account group as needed. 

Filtering on the account group restricts the information displayed to certain dimensions. 

Before you define filtering for the account group, consider how you will use it in reports, 

and if filtering on the account group would be too restrictive. In most cases, we recommend 

filtering on the report or graph instead of on the account group. Filtering on the report or 

graph at runtime increases flexibility in reporting. 

Filtering on the account group limits selection to one member of a dimension. If you need 

more than one member of a dimension—for example, multiple vendors or 

customers—filtering at the report level allows you to use dimension groups that contain 

multiple members of the same dimension. 

Consider who will see the report. For reports that you create for a single audience and are 

sure that the information needed is always the same, filtering by account group could be 

your best option. 

For reports with multiple audiences, filtering at the report or graph level might be a better 

solution. In this way, you can create one report and filter it at runtime so that the report 

displays the information based on who will receive the report, for example, different 

department managers or business unit leaders. With this method, you can set report 

filtering without having to create multiple account groups and reports. 

---


<!-- Page 90 -->
Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

About the Structure Type  

Every account group has a default method of calculating amounts in reports, based on structure 

type. For example, account groups that contain income or expense accounts are typically 

calculated “for the period.” Calculation methods can be: 

• 
For the period: Calculate only the amounts in the period you selected. 

• 
Start of period: Calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the start of the selected period. 

• 
End of period: Calculate the amounts cumulatively up to the end of the selected period. 

Computation structures use a mathematical formula for calculation between account groups. 

These calculation methods enable you to create formulas for your account group. You can add, 

subtract, multiply, or divide one side of the formula by the other, then display the amount results 

as a ratio, percentage, or average, based on the formula that you select. 

Calculation structures are basically equations. They require two accounts, account groups, or 

constant numbers. Each side of the formula is an account, account group, or constant number. For 

example, you could: 

• 
Divide the revenue G/L account by the employee statistical account to obtain your revenue 

per employee. 

• 
Divide your total debt by your current assets to get the debt ratio. 

You can use an account group of type “Computation” to define a mathematical computation that 

you want to include as a section in financial reports. For example, you might want to include a 

section called “Revenue per Employee,” where the amount is computed by dividing revenue by 

the number of employees. 

Unlike computations that appear in the columns of a financial report, a computation account 

group enables you to add a computation to report rows. 

For Multi-Entity Shared Companies 

The account group is a master object that can either be common (that is, accessible) to all entities, 

or exclusive (private) to a specific entity. Learn more about account groups in a multi-entity 

shared environment (on page 59).

Structure Type Section 

Structure Type 
Select the structure type. The group members you add are based on the 

structure type. 

• 
Accounts: Contains individual accounts or ranges of accounts. 

• 
Group of account groups: Contains a collection of account 

groups. 

• 
Statistical accounts: Contains statistical accounts or ranges of 

statistical accounts. 

• 
Computation: Contains other account groups or individual 

accounts that you use as components in a mathematical 

equation; results of the equation display in your financial report. 

• 
Category: Is based on the category assigned to accounts rather 

than a specific list of accounts. 

• 
Statistical category: Is based on the statistical category assigned 

to accounts rather than a specific list of accounts. 
Each structure type is designed for a specific use and has different features. After you assign a structure type to an account group, it can’t be changed.If the wrong structure was assigned to the account group but the account group isn
’t being used by a report yet, then delete the group 
and recreate it, assigning it the correct structure type.

Calculation method 
Select the period: 

• 
For period: Select this option to calculate only the amounts in 

the period you selected. 

• 
Start of period: Select this option to calculate the amounts 

cumulatively up to the start of the selected period (Start of 

period). 

• 
End of period: Select this option to calculate the amounts 

cumulatively up to the end of the selected period. 

The latter is useful, for example, in a balance-forward situation. 

Set up balance sheet account groups as End of Period. Set up 

income statement account groups as For period. 
The Calculation method drop-down list appears only if you selected 
Accounts, Statistical accounts, Category, or Statistical category as the structure type.

Account Group Managers 

The Account group manager is the person who should be consulted about changing the account 

group. The person can be a fellow staff member or a partner who implemented the account 

group. 

If you’re using the account group manager option, consider adding a view of Account groups that 
inc ludes the Account group manager.

Creating an Account Group Manager 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`


Create an account group manager directly from the Account group information page. 

To create an account group manager: 

1. 
In the General Ledger menu, select the Account Groups option. 

2. 
Find the account group that you want to assign a new account group manager. 

3. 
Click the Edit link. 

4. 
Type the name of the manager in the Account group manager field. Account groups can 

have only one manager, but you can add the same manager to multiple account groups. 

5. 
Click Save. 
In multi-entity companies, you can assign the 
Account group managerat both the top and 
entity levels.

Editing an Account Group Manager 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`


As managers change positions or leave, you can update an account group manager directly from 

the Account group information page. 

1. 
In the General Ledger menu, select the Account Groups option. 

2. 
Find the account group you want to assign to a different account group manager. 

3. 
In the Account group manager field, clear the existing name, and then type the new 

account group manager name. 

4. 
Click Save. 

---

Deleting an Account Group Manager 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups`


To delete an account group manager from an account group: 

1. 
In the General Ledger menu, select the Account Groups option. 

2. 
Locate the account group that has a manager that you want to delete. 

3. 
Clear the text in the Account group manager field. 

4. 
Click Save. 

Adding an Account Group to a Financial Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Financial Reports > Edit > Rows`


Add an account group when you want to build sections and rows in your report.  

To add accounts to an account group: 

1. 
Open the General ledger menu. 

2. 
Select the Financial reports option. 

3. 
Find the report you want and click the Edit link. 

4. 
Click the Rows tab. 

5. 
Click the Select Account groups button. The Account group information box opens. 

6. 
In the Account group purpose drop-down list, select the purpose of the account group. 

7. 
In the Account group drop-down list, select an account group.  

8. 
Click the Include in report button to add the account group to the report. 

9. 
Repeat steps 7 and 8 to add more account groups.  

10. 
When you have included the account groups that you want, click the OK button.

##### Installing System Account Groups
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 102*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Installing System Account Groups 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Search & Find > Account Groups > Account 
Groups Library`


You can install system-defined account groups from the Account Groups library. We recommend 

that you create default account groups now to save time later, and so that you can obtain 

financial statements with greater ease later on. If this isn’t possible, you can install the system 

account groups from the Account Groups library. 

To install system account groups: 

1. 
Open the General ledger menu. 

2. 
In the Search & Find section, select Account groups. The Account groups list opens. 

3. 
Click the Account groups library button. The list of installable system account groups 

opens. 

4. 
Find the desired account group that you want to install, and then click the Install link, or to 

install all account groups, you can click Install all.  

The system installs the account group using the name of the account group, without the 

System_ prefix. For example, if you install System_Amortization Expense, the account group 

is installed with the account group name “Amortization Expense.” 

5. 
When the account groups have been installed, click Close to close the confirmation window. 

---

Account Groups Hierarchy Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Account Groups > Hierarchy`


Use the Account groups hierarchy report to display a graphical representation of the hierarchy of 

your account groups, which includes member account groups and their accounts. The lists of 

account groups and accounts are sorted in ascending alphabetical order. If an account group has 

no members, only the accounts are displayed which helps you identify groups that have not been 

mapped to a group. 

To display the account groups hierarchy report: 

1. 
Open the General ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Account groups. 

3. 
Find the account group for which you want to view the report, and then click the Hierarchy 

link.

##### Customizing and Running the Account Groups Hierarchy Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 104*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide

Customizing and Running the Account Groups Hierarchy Report 

If you want to see every result for a particular category, do not make selections from any of the 

data entry boxes. You can run this report without entering any search information. If you do that, 

however, the report may take a long time to run and may return too many results to be useful. 

Before running the report, you can select the types of items to include and specify how you want 

the report to appear. 

To customize and run an Accounts group hierarchy report: 

1. 
From the Applications menu, select General ledger, and then select the All tab. 

2. 
Select Account groups. 

3. 
Find the account group for which you want to view the report, and then click the Hierarchy 

link. The report is displayed. 

4. 
Click the Customize button. 

Set the Filters  

Set the filters to get the specific data you want. 

1. 
Select one of the following options to focus the report on the set of account groups either 

you or the system created: 

• 
User Defined Account Groups 

• 
System Account Groups 

These choices are available for companies that were initially created with a setup template 

that automatically generated categories mapped to account groups and standard financial 

reports based on the account groups. 

2. 
To filter by a specific type of account group, select the type of account group to include in 

the report in the Account group type drop-down list. 

3. 
To filter by a specific range of account groups, select the account group to include in the 

report in the Account group from drop-down list. Do the same in the Account group to 

drop-down list. 

4. 
To filter by a specific range of accounts, select the starting account to include in the report 

in the Account number from drop-down list, and then select the ending account in the 

Account number to drop-down list. 

5. 
To filter by a specific range of categories, select the starting category to include in the 

report in the Category from drop-down list. Select the ending category in the Category to 

drop-down list. 

The Category field i s available for companies that were initially created with a setup 
template.

Format the Report 

To change the page layout, click the Page orientation drop-down field, and then select either 

Portrait or Landscape. Portrait is the default layout. 

• 
Portrait: Display or print the report pages vertically. 

• 
Landscape: Display or print the report pages horizontally. 

Filling In the Titles Section and Run the Report 

Use this section to add a title, subtitle, and footer to your report. Titles, subtitles, and footers 

apply only to the PDF version of this report (click Print to get the PDF version). 

The system completes the Report title 1 field with the system-assigned report name. This title 

displays in large type at the top of the page directly under your company name. 

1. 
You can leave the title as the default or change it by typing over the default name in Report 

title 1. 

2. 
In Report title 2, enter the text that you want to appear under the title of the report. This 

subtitle displays in smaller type, directly beneath the main title. 

3. 
In Footer text, type the text that you want to appear in the footer of the report. Footer text 

displays at the bottom center of the report. 
You can use up to 80 characters, including spaces, in each title, subtitle, and footer.

4. 
Click the View button to run the report.  
View, process, memorize, export, or customize the report or add it to the dashboard by clicking one of the buttons at the top of the page. You can go back and forth between customizing the report and running the report to get the precise results you want. Fill in the desired fields, and then click View to see the report. If after viewing the results you 
want to refine the report further, click Customize to return to the report setup page. 
Repeat this process until you’re satisfied. Als
o, for complex financial reports you plan to run regularly, save the report when you are done setting it up. The next time you are ready to run the report, change only the As of date before running the report.

##### Viewing a Monthly Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 106*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Viewing a Monthly Report 

On the report customization page, click View. 

The report displays all results for the default reporting period. 

To refine the report, click the Customize button. 

Reports have two kinds of settings, persisting and non-persisting. 

Persisting settings retain any changes you make from session to session. When you return to this 

page to run this report again, each setting displays exactly as you set it before. For example, if you 

type “My Latest Report” into the Report title 1 field, the next time you run this report, the Report 

title 1 box displays My latest report. 

Persisting settings include: 

• 
Settings in the Format section 

• 
Settings in the Titles section 

• 
Check boxes in the Filters section 

Non-persisting settings do not retain your changes. Instead, every time you return to this page to 

run this report or chart, the settings are set to the system defaults. If you changed these settings 

the last time you ran this report, you will have to change them again. 

Non-persisting settings include: 

• 
Settings in the Time Period section 

• 
Drop-down lists in the Filters section 
Not all reports include all of the above sections.

---



##### Account Groups Hierarchy Report Columns
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 107*

Chapter 2: Search & Find 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Account Groups Hierarchy Report Columns 

The main headings in this report are taken from the account name as entered on the G/L account 

group Information page or the G/L account information page. This is different from other 

financial reports, which typically use the heading title, and then the name if no title is specified. 

The reason the name is used here is so that you can more easily compare the names in this report 

to the names in the Account groups list. 

The divisions in the report are differentiated by whether they are top-level groups that contain 

other member groups, and if they are groups that contain only accounts or categories. Any group 

that contains only accounts or categories is displayed after the top-level groups. The hierarchical 


> **Navigation Path:** `organization of account groups is controlled via General Ledger > All > Financial report`


structures > [member link] > Account group members. 

If your company was created with a Quick Start Financials setup template 

The report operates slightly differently for companies that use a Quick Start Financials setup 

template in that the setup template creates a set of system account groups in addition to your 

own account groups. 

If your company was initially created with a setup template, a set of standard account groups was 

copied to your company. Consequently, a filter lets you choose to view the system account 

groups or the user-defined ones that your company created. You may navigate into user-defined 

account groups by clicking a link in the report, but you can’t navigate into system-generated 

account groups. 

---



##### Buttons
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 108*

Search & Find 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Buttons 

Customize 
Opens the report’s customization page to change filters and time periods 

and to exclude specific types of data. 

The Customize button appears only after viewing a report or ledger.

View 
Opens the report in your browser. 

Print 
Displays the report in printable PDF format so you can print it or save it on 

your computer. 

Process & store 
Generates the report and stores it for later access. 

Available formats include HTML, printable (PDF), Excel, CSV 

(comma-separated value), and Text. You can store reports in the system, 

and then access them with the My stored reports option in the menu.  

Email 
Sends the report as an email message. Click to open the Email report box, 

and then fill in the To field with the recipient’s email address. 
This button is not available for all reports.

Add to dashboard 
Adds the report to a dashboard. 

Select the dashboard that you want to add it to, type a name for the 

report, and then type a description of the report.  

Select the Public check box to make the report available on a dashboard. 
This option is not available for all reports. 

Memorize 
Memorizes (that is, saves) the report in the system so you can refer to it 

later or modify and re-run it. 
Reports that you memorize can be scheduled to run on a recurring basis and can be automatically emailed.

Export 
Exports the report to a separate file that you can then distribute or save on 

your computer. 
The file formats for exporting report data vary by report.

Select 
Opens the multi-select box, where you can select items to filter by. 

In the multi-select box, select the items that you want to include in the 

report.  
The option to multi-select items is not available for all reports.

---

#### Trial Balance Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 110*

Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Trial Balance Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Trial Balance`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Trial Balance`


Use the Trial Balance report to view balances of all accounts.  
The Trial Balance 
report is a standard report. If you are looking for information on financial reports, see Creating a Financial Report.

When running this report, you can: 

• 
Include or exclude statistical accounts. 

• 
Filter the list to specific locations and departments, and filter by accounts and account 

groups. 

• 
Display only accounts with a zero balance or only zero-balance accounts that have had 

activity.  

• 
Show accumulated balances for income statement accounts that close into retained 

earnings at year-end. 

• 
Include or exclude the Debits and Credits columns. 

• 
Export the report to an Excel XLS file. 
We recommend saving any complex financial reports that you plan to run regularly. The next time you run the report, you will need to change only the As of datefield.

##### Customizing and Running the Trial Balance Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 112*

Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Customizing and Running the Trial Balance Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Daily Tasks > G/L Journals > Trial Balance 
Report`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Daily Tasks > Trial Balance Report`


To customize the Trial Balance report: 

1. 
Open the menu of one of these applications: General Ledger, Forecasting & Planning, or 

Global Consolidations. 

2. 
In the Daily Tasks section, select the Trial Balance report option. 

If you opened the Global Consolidations menu, select Trial Balance from the Reports 

column of the menu. 

Filling In the Reporting Accounts Labels Section 

If you enabled reporting account labels in the General Ledger, you can substitute the account 

labels with the reporting account set’s labels in the report. For more information, see Reporting 

Account Sets. 

1. 
To enable reporting account labels, select the Substitute with reporting accounts labels 

check box. 

2. 
In the Reporting account set drop-down list, select the reporting account set whose labels 

you want to substitute into the report.  

3. 
If you prefer to choose the reporting account set each time that you run the report, then 

select the Prompt on run check box. When you select this option, the system asks you for 

the reporting account set every time you run the report. 

Filling In the Time Period Section 

Define the time period for the report. You can either select the Reporting period and As of date, 

or select a balance date range to identify the report’s coverage period, but not both. If you do not 

specify a reporting period, the system generates a report for the current month to date.  

To run a report for a Reporting period: 

1. 
In the Reporting period drop-down list, select the Reporting period. 

2. 
In the As of date field, select the date to begin the report coverage.  
Type “ p ”in a date field to automatically insert the last day of the prior month. See 
Specifying Dates in Getting Started for a full list of shortcut keys related to enteringdates.

3. 
Select the Prompt on run check box if you want to be able to choose the reporting period 

and As of date when you run the report. 

---


<!-- Page 113 -->
Chapter 3: Daily Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

To run a report by balance date range: 

• 
Specify the Opening balance date and Closing balance date for the report’s period of 

coverage.  

Filling In the Filters Section 

To filter the Trial Balance report: 

1. 
In the Reporting book drop-down list, select the desired reporting book, Accrual or Cash. 

2. 
If your company has multiple reporting books, click the Select button and then use the 

multi-select box to select the Other books that you want to use for this report. 
The list of journals you can include in the report is restricted to journals for those reporting books.

3. 
To combine the reporting book and other books, select the Combine reporting book with 

other books check box. 

Learn more about the reporting book options: 

• 
If you opted for dual-method reporting (Accrual and Cash) or if you are using Global 

Consolidations, you can change the main Reporting Book. For Accrual-only companies, 

the Accrual book is automatically selected. 

• 
If you enabled compliance, tax, or user-defined books, you can choose to include 

those Other Books, with or without the main reporting book. 

4. 
In the G/L Account Number Selection section, select the account, range of accounts, or 

account group to cover in the report. 

• 
To specify a range of accounts, select the Range option and then choose the starting 

account in the Account from drop-down list, and then select the ending account in 

the Account to drop-down list. 

• 
To select multiple non-contiguous accounts, select the Multiple accounts option and 

then click the Select accounts link. The Select multiple accounts box opens so that 

you can select the accounts that you want in the report. Click the Save button when 

your list of accounts is complete. 

• 
To include all accounts in the report, select the All accounts option. 

• 
To include all accounts in an account group, select the desired account group from the 

Account group drop-down list.  
If you want users to be able to run this report in Spend Management Site Experience (Site View), then select the Site View Financial Account option as the account group. Be sure 
that this account group has the members that your business needs require. You can edit this account group to add or change its members as needed. General ledger > Account 

Groups > Edit

---


<!-- Page 114 -->
Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Filling In the Segment Section 

This section appears when you have segments (on page 407) enabled in your system. 

To limit the report by segments: 

• 
Select one or more segments from the Segments drop-down list.  

• 
You can also select all values for a segment type by selecting the Select All check box. 

If the Segments drop
-down list does not appear, then your system does not have the 
segments feature enabled(on page 407).

Filling In the Dimension Filters Section 

From the drop-down lists, select the dimensions to include in your report. The list of dimensions 

to filter on may vary in your system, based on your system’s dimension configuration. You can 

configure dimensions on the Configure General Ledger Page. 

1. 
Select a department or department group from the Department drop-down list to limit the 

report by department. 

2. 
Select the Include subs check box to include subdimensions (child departments) when 

filtering. Clear this check box if you don’t want the system to include children of the 

department dimension when filtering. 

3. 
Select the Individual report check box to create a separate report for each department.  

4. 
If you would prefer to select the Department when the report runs, select the Prompt on 

run check box. 

---


<!-- Page 115 -->
Chapter 3: Daily Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

5. 
To limit the report to a selected property (location) or location group, click the Select button 

under the Property label, and then select a property or group from the Selection box. After 

choosing a couple of properties, the Select button is replaced with the list of the properties 

that you chose: 

6. 
Select the Include subs check box to include sub dimensions (child locations) when filtering. 

Clear this check box if you don’t want the system to include children of the location 

dimension when filtering. 

7. 
Select the Individual report check box to create a separate report for each location.  
At the end of the report setup, you must select the Process and Store button when you 
choose the Individual reportcheck box.

8. 
If you prefer to choose the Location when the report runs, select the Prompt on run check 

box. 

9. 
To limit the report by one unit, select the unit from the Unit drop-down list. 
If your company has enabled additional dimensions, the Vendor and 
Customer drop
- down 
lists may appear below the Unitdrop
-down list. 

10. 
In the Vendor drop-down list, select a vendor or vendor group to limit the report by vendor. 

11. 
Select the Include subs check box to include subdimensions (child vendors) when filtering. 

Clear this check box if you don’t want the system to include children of the vendor 

dimension when filtering. 

12. 
To limit the report by vendor type, select a vendor type in the Vendor type drop-down list. 

13. 
To limit the report by customer, select a customer or customer group in the Customer 

drop-down list. 

14. 
Select the Include subs check box to include subdimensions (child customers) when 

filtering. Clear this check box if you don’t want the system to include children of the 

customer dimension when filtering. 

15. 
To limit the report by customer type, select a customer type in the Customer type 

drop-down list. 
At the end of the report setup, you must select the Process and Store button when you 
cho ose the 
Individual reportcheck box.

<!-- Page 116 -->
Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide

Filling In the Format Section 

Use the Format section to determine the format of the report. 

1. 
If you want to include dimension values in the report, click the Select button in the Show 

dimension values section, and then use the multi-select box to choose the dimension 

attributes that you want to show in the report. 

2. 
To include a column for tax codes in the report, select the Include tax codes check box. 

3. 
To include accounts with a zero balance: 

• 
Select the All option in the Show zero balance accounts section.  

• 
To include accounts with a zero balance only when they are active, select the Only 

with activity option.  

• 
To exclude accounts with a zero balance, select the Do not show option. 

4. 
The Show accounts section determines how to handle statistical account data: 

• 
To include statistical accounts in the report, select the Including statistical accounts 

option. 

• 
To limit the report so that it shows only statistical accounts, select the Only showing 

statistical accounts option.  

• 
To exclude statistical accounts from appearing in the report, select the Do not show 

statistical accounts option.  

5. 
Select the Retain YTD Balances check box to display the accumulated balances for IS 

accounts for the fiscal year as of the beginning of the period. 

6. 
Select the Show adjustments check box to show the Adjusting debit and Adjusting credit 

columns in the Trial Balance report. Clear the check box to hide the two columns. 

7. 
To exclude the debit and credit columns from the report, select the Remove Debit and 

Credit columns check box. 

8. 
To include the Net change column to show the difference between debits and credits, select 

the Show net change column check box. 

9. 
Select the desired page orientation for the report. 

Filling In the Titles Section 

Use this section to add a title, subtitle, and footer to your report. Titles, subtitles, and footers 

apply only to the PDF version of this report (click Print to get the PDF version). 

The system completes the Report title 1 field with the system-assigned name of the report. This 

title appears in large type at the top of the page directly below your company name. You can 

leave the title as the default or change it.  

1. 
To change the title, type over the default name that the system automatically supplied.

##### Trial Balance Report Columns
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 117*

Chapter 3: Daily Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

2. 
In the Report title 2 field, type the text that you want to appear below the title of the report. 

This subtitle appears in smaller type, directly beneath the main title. 

3. 
In the Footer text field, type the text that you want to appear in the footer of the report. 

Footer text appears at the bottom of the report. 
You can use up to 80 characters, including spaces, in each title, subtitle, and footer.

4. 
Click the View button to run the report.  

What’s Next?  After you run the report, you can email the report to others. Use trial and error to get the precise results you want by filling in the desired fields and then clicking 
View to run the 
report. If you want to refine the report further, click Customize to re turn to the report 
setup page. Repeat this process as needed.

Trial Balance Report Columns 

Click any link to navigate into the details. The default Trial Balance report contains the following 

columns. 

Columns 

Account number 
This is the account number. 

Account name 
This is the account name. 

Opening Balance 
This is the amount of the account’s balance on the date shown. 

Debit 
These are charges posted to the account for the selected period. 

Credit 
These are payments posted to the account for the selected period. 

Adj Credit 
These are adjustments made to the payments posted to the account for 

the selected period. 
This column appears in the report when the Show Adjustmentscheck 
box is selected.

Adj Debit 
These are adjustments made to the charges posted to the account for 

the selected period. 
This column appears in the report when the Show Adjustmentscheck 
box is selected.

Closing Balance 
This is the total amount of the account’s balance after adjustments.

#### General Ledger Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 119*

Chapter 3: Daily Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

General Ledger Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Daily Tasks > General Ledger Report`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > General Ledger`


Use the General Ledger report to view data in your General Ledger accounts. 
The General Ledger 
report is a standard report. If you are looking for information on financial reports, see Creating a Financial Report.

When running this report, you can: 

• 
Filter the data by single account, account range, account group, or multiple accounts. 

• 
Include normal G/L accounts and statistical accounts. 

• 
Constrain all of these by location and department. 

• 
Sort by location and by department to display the corresponding subtotals. 

• 
Show accumulated balances for IS accounts that close into retained earnings at year-end. 

• 
View the report in detail mode to see the transactions. 

• 
Display or hide subtotals. 

By default, this report shows only accounts with balances greater than zero. However, you can 

choose to include zero-balance accounts. 

Summary Version

Filling In the Format Section 

1. 
Click the Select button of Show dimension values, and then select the dimension values to 

add to the report. If a user has already selected dimension values, the Show dimension 

values box is displayed instead of a Select button. For information, see Using the 

multi-select box.  

2. 
In the Show zero balance accounts section, select an option: 

• 
All: Select this option to include all accounts, regardless of their balance. 

• 
Only with activity: Select this option to include all accounts where there is activity in 

the reported period. 

• 
Do not show: Select this option to include only accounts with balances greater than 

zero. 

3. 
In the Show transaction presentation section, select an option: 

• 
Summary: Select this option to display the report with summarized columns in 

landscape orientation. 

• 
Details: Select this option to display the report with line-item details—including 

beginning and ending balance, debits, credits, and adjustments—in portrait 

orientation. 

4. 
In the Select doc column value section, select an option: 

• 
Reference number: Select this option to display the reference number. If there is no 

reference number entered, the system displays the A/P invoice, A/R invoice, or 

adjustment number. 

• 
AP/AR invoice/Adjustment number: Select this option to display the A/P invoice, A/R 

invoice, or adjustment number (for example, the check number for the check used to 

pay the A/P invoice). If there is no A/P invoice, A/R invoice, or adjustment number 

entered, the system displays the reference number. 
This option is available only when your system’s accounting method is set to Accrual 
and the Show transaction informationoption is set to Details.

5. 
To display only departments and locations with some activity in the current period, select 

the Show department/location with activity check box. 

Departments/Locations with no activity in this period display only if you clear this check box. 

6. 
To determine the net loss on an account without doing the math yourself, select the Show 

net change check box. 

In your report, you will see an additional line that shows the net change for each account for 

the period on which you are reporting. More specifically, the net change is the account total 

plus debits minus credits. If you selected the prior year as the period, it is the net change for 

the prior year.

7.

each line for CSV export check box.

the transaction in one line of the report. This option is available when you select the Details 

option in the Show transaction presentation section.

when you select the Details option in the Show transaction presentation section. 

When you select the Show journal transaction date check box, the name of the “Posting 

date” column changes to “Posting period” in the General Ledger report. The Posting period 

is the month and year that the transaction was posted.

an A/P journal transaction. When a payment was made in multiple payments, all payment 

numbers are included in the column and are separated by commas. This option is available 

when you select the Details option in the Show transaction presentation section.

payment column check box, then the system adds the

basis detailed General ledger report.

number in the Doc column instead of a document number. This option applies only to cash 

basis reports.

Payment date column to the detailed version of the report.

14. 
To move the account information to a separate column, select the Show account in column 

check box. This change makes it easy to extract the report and pivot it in Excel. When 

selected, the system adds Account number and Account name columns to the report, and 

removes the row with the Account number, Account name, and the Total row. When there 

is no activity for the report’s period, the Debit and Credit columns show 0.00 amounts.

1. 
Select the page orientation. 

2. 
To fit the report to the width of a page for ease of printing, select the Fit to one page wide 

for printing check box. 

3. 
In the Sort by drop-down list, select how you want to sort the report. You can sort by date, 

department name, department ID, location name, location ID, vendor name, vendor ID, 

partner name, customer name, customer ID, industry name, industry ID, intercompany 

segment name, intercompany segment ID, and unit. 

Filling In the Titles Section 

Use this section to add a title, subtitle, and footer to your report. Titles, subtitles, and footers 

apply only to the PDF version of this report (click Print to get the PDF version). 

The system completes the Report title 1 field with the system-assigned name of the report. This 

title appears in large type at the top of the page directly below your company name. You can 

leave the title as the default or change it.  

1. 
To change the title, type over the default name that the system automatically supplied. 

2. 
In the Report title 2 field, type the text that you want to appear below the title of the report. 

This subtitle appears in smaller type, directly beneath the main title. 

3. 
In the Footer text field, type the text that you want to appear in the footer of the report.

Footer text appears at the bottom of the report. 
You can use up to 80 characters, including spaces, in each title, subtitle, and footer.

4. 
Click the View button to run the report.  

What’s Next?  

After you run the report, you can either email the report to others or return to the report setup 

page to refine the results. 

• 
To email the report to others, click the Email button. 

• 
To return to the main report page and refine the report further, click the Customize button. 

You can use trial and error to get the precise results you want by filling in the desired fields and 

then clicking View to see the report. After you view the results, if you want to refine the report 

further, then click Customize to return to the report setup page. Repeat this process until you’re 

satisfied with the resulting report. For complex financial reports that you plan to run regularly, 

save the report when you are done setting it up. The next time you are ready to run the report, 

change the As of date before running the report.

##### General Ledger Report Columns
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 130*

Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

General Ledger Report Columns 

Click any link to navigate to the details. The summary General Ledger report contains the 

following columns: 

Summary Report 

Posted dt (link) 
This is the date on which the transaction was posted to the G/L. Click the 

link to open the journal entries associated with this date. 

If you selected the Show journal transaction date check box, the name 

of this column changes to Posting period. The Posting period is the 

month and year that the transaction was posted. 

Source post date 
This is the original posting date of the entry. When your system is 

configured to advance the G/L posting date, you can refer to this field 

for the original posting date of the entry. This is a read-only field. The 

system stores the original posting date when you post the journal entry. 

This field appears only when Advance G/L posting is enabled for the 

associated entity. Contact your RealPage Support Representative to 

enable the Advance post date feature. 

Doc 
This is a document number associated with the transaction.  

When your system’s accounting method is Cash and you select the 

Show document number as invoice number option in the report’s 

settings, this column displays the invoice number instead. 

When your system’s accounting method is Accrual, the contents of this 

column is determined by the Select doc column value setting, reference 

number, or AP/AR invoice/adjustment number.  

• 
When you choose Reference number, then this column contains 

the reference number associated with the transaction. When no 

reference number is entered, the system displays the A/P invoice, 

A/R invoice, or adjustment number. 

• 
When you choose AP/AR invoice/Adjustment number, this 

column contains the A/P invoice, A/R invoice, or adjustment 

number, such as the number of the check used to pay the A/P 

invoice. If the transaction has no A/P invoice, A/R invoice, or 

adjustment number, then the column contains the reference 

number. 

---


<!-- Page 131 -->
Chapter 3: Daily Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Memo/Description 
If you included text in the description and line-item memo fields when 

you created a journal entry, then the description or memo text appears 

in this column. 

When you enter a description, that text is automatically included as the 

line-item memo entry, but can be overwritten. This is usually a 

description of the transaction, such as the type of invoice, date, and type 

of summary. 

Department 
This is the department number associated with the account; for example, 

900. 

Location 
This is the location associated with the account. 

Unit 
This is the unit at the location that is associated with the account. 

JNL 
This is the name of the application from which this transaction was 

derived; for example, A/P. 

Curr 
This is the currency in which the transaction was charged or paid. 

Txn amt 
This is the amount of the transaction. 

Debit (link) 
These are the charges posted to the account for the selected period. 

Click to view the journal entry associated with the charge. 

Credit (link) 
These are the payments posted to the account for the selected period. 

Click to view the journal entry associated with the payment. 

Balance 
This is the total amount of the account’s balance for the selected period. 

Total net change 
This is the total net change between the start date and end date of the 

report. 

When you select the Details option in the Show transaction presentation section, the system adds 

these columns to the report: 

Detailed Report  

Doc dt 
This is the date on the document associated with the transaction. This 

column appears only when you select the detailed version of the report. 

Payment no 
This is the payment number associated with an A/P journal transaction. 

When a payment was made in multiple payments, all payment numbers 

are included in the column and are separated by commas. This option is 

available when you select the Details option in the Show transaction 

presentation section.

##### Buttons
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 132*

Daily Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Buttons 

Customize 
Opens the report’s customization page to change filters and time periods 

and to exclude specific types of data. 

The Customize button appears only after viewing a report or ledg
er.

View 
Opens the report in your browser. 

Print 
Displays the report in printable PDF format so you can print it or save it on 

your computer. 

Process & store 
Generates the report and stores it for later access. 

Available formats include HTML, printable (PDF), Excel, CSV 

(comma-separated value), and Text. You can store reports in the system, 

and then access them with the My stored reports option in the menu.  

Email 
Sends the report as an email message. Click to open the Email report box, 

and then fill in the To field with the recipient’s email address. 
This button is not available for all reports.

Add to dashboard 
Adds the report to a dashboard. 

Select the dashboard that you want to add it to, type a name for the 

report, and then type a description of the report.  

Select the Public check box to make the report available on a dashboard. 
This option is not available for all reports. 

Memorize 
Memorizes (that is, saves) the report in the system so you can refer to it 

later or modify and re-run it. 
Reports that you memorize can be scheduled to run on a recurring basis and can be automatically emailed.

Export 
Exports the report to a separate file that you can then distribute or save on 

your computer. 
The file formats for exporting report data vary by report.

Select 
Opens the multi-select box, where you can select items to filter by. 

In the multi-select box, select the items that you want to include in the 

report.  
The option to multi-select items is not available for all reports.

##### Creating a Budget Manually
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 137*

Chapter 4: Periodic Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Creating a Budget Manually 

Manually creating a budget allows you to enter budget data for each account one by one, month 

by month. Manual budget creation is useful if you are adding budget data for only some of your 

accounts. 

Step 1: Create a new budget and give it a unique Budget ID 

Before you can upload a budget spreadsheet or manually enter budget data, you must first create 

the budget. 

1. 
Create a new budget and give it a Budget ID. Best practice is to give the budget an ID that 

doesn’t reference a particular fiscal year or date. Then you can append new time periods to 

your existing budget. 

2. 
Select Create a new budget. 

3. 
If this is to be a consolidated budget, enable the check box and enter a consolidated 

currency. 

4. 
If this is the default budget to use, enable the check box by that feature. 

Step 2: Add budget information for your accounts  

After you’ve created and named your budget, you’re ready to add budget data for your accounts. 

When you’re manually adding budget data, you can only enter data for only one account at a 

time.  
If you’re tracking budget data at the dimension level, such as tracking data for different departments and locations, you
’ll need to add multiple entries for each account. For example, if 
you are entering Travel Expense data for three locations, you’ll need three entries for this 
account.

To enter budget information for an account: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu. 

2. 
Select Budgets. 

3. 
Click the Budget details link for the budget. 

4. 
Click the Add button. 

5. 
On the Budget amount details page, add general information about the budget for the 

account. 

• 
Select the Account for which you’re entering budget data. 

• 
Optionally, select dimensions for your budget data. For example, if you are entering 

budget data for a particular location, select the Location.  

6. 
Use Periods from date and Periods to date to select reporting periods for the budget 

period you are creating. You can select a single period or a range of periods. 

---


<!-- Page 138 -->
Periodic Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

7. 
Click the Show periods button to display the reporting periods. 

When you click Show periods, the page refreshes to display both the reporting periods you 


> **Navigation Path:** `can include in the budget and the fields for entering individual amounts. 
If no reporting periods display when you click Show periods, go to 
General Ledger > Setup`


> More > Reporting periods. For the reporting period to use, click 
Edit to display the 
Reporting Period Information page, and select the 
Use for budgeting check box. 

8. 
Enter budget amounts for the reporting periods. (See the next section.) 

Step 3: Enter budget amounts 

There are two methods to assign a budget amount to a reporting period. You can enter the 

amounts manually or you can have the amounts computed for you. 

Option 1: Enter amounts manually  

1. 
Select the check box to the left of the Reporting period to activate it. 

2. 
Type amounts into the Budget amount fields for each budget period. 

3. 
Optionally add a note about the budget or budget amount. 

4. 
After the data has been entered for this account, click Save. 

Option 2: Automatically calculate budget data based on amounts from a past period  

1. 
Select the period you want to use as the starting point for the calculation from the Past 

periods drop-down list. 

2. 
Select a method from the Based on drop-down list. You can choose to base the calculation 

on Actual data or Budget data.  

The choices are described below. 

• 
Actuals: This option retrieves the actual amount for the selected past period. The 

system calculates budget values by changing the actual value for the selected past 

period by the amount or percentage entered in the Grow by field. The Compute 

amount field is not required. 

• 
Budget: This option retrieves the budget amount for the selected past period. The 

system calculates budget values by changing the past period value by the amount or 

percentage entered in the Grow by field. The Compute amount field is not required. 

3. 
To add reporting periods to your budget, select the check box for the Reporting periods at 

the bottom of the page. 

4. 
Click the Compute button to calculate the amounts for the selected periods. 

5. 
Click the Save button. 

---



##### Creating a Budget by Modifying an Existing Budget
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 139*

Chapter 4: Periodic Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

What’s Next? 

You can have the system warn users who are creating A/P invoices and purchase orders when the 

amount exceeds the budget. You can also have the system prevent users from creating the 

transactions when the amount exceeds the budget (on page 155). 

You can also grant permissions to some users to allow them to bypass the budget check.  

Creating a Budget by Modifying an Existing Budget 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Budgets`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Budgets`


Creating a new budget based on one that already exists is a quick way to get your budget process 

started.  

When you create a budget from one that already exists, you can include a range of reporting 

periods from the previous year, enabling you to replicate and retain a year’s worth of account 

budgets instantly. 

Before You Begin 

 Before you can add data, create a new budget, and then give it a unique Budget ID. 

To create a budget based on an existing budget: 

1. 
In the Budgets list, locate the budget you want use as the basis for your new budget and 

click the Export link. 

Exporting to a CSV retains the correct field IDs for upload. 

2. 
Update the CSV file using Microsoft Excel or other spreadsheet program. 

• 
Spreadsheet programs enable you to quickly replicate rows and columns, and even 

automatically change the data.  

• 
After the data in the Excel file is changed, it must be saved again as a CSV file. 
Be sure to update the Budget_ID column with the new 
Budget ID that you created in Step 
1.

3. 
Click Import in the Budgets list to upload the budget data. 

---

*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 140*



##### Viewing Budget Details

Viewing Existing Budgets

Budgets can be viewed at the account level. An entire budget can also be exported and viewed in

You can view all of the data budgeted for an account for each reporting period in a single list. You 

can also see individual account detail with dimension data. 

To view budget details for an account:

• 
The budget amounts list appears, showing rows for all accounts that have been 

budgeted.  

• 
If you are using dimensions in your budgets, you’ll have more than one row per 

account. For example, you might see one row for Account 4000 for the New York 

location and another row for Account 4000 for the California location. 

2. 
Click the Budgets link next to the budget you want to view.  

The Budget periods list opens with the budget data for this account for each reporting 

period. 

3. 
To view additional details for a reporting period, click the View link.

In the Budgets list, click the Budget details link in the row with the budget that you want to 

see. The Budget details list opens.  

2. 
Click the Budgets link next to the account containing the budget that you want to view.  

The Budget periods list opens.

In the row with the account that you want to view, click the View link. The Budget amount 

details page opens.

##### Exporting a Budget to Be Viewed in a Spreadsheet
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 141*



##### Modifying a Budget Manually
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 141*

Chapter 4: Periodic Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Exporting a Budget to Be Viewed in a Spreadsheet 

To export a budget to be viewed in a spreadsheet:  

1. 
Select the Budgets menu option. 

2. 
In the Budgets list, locate the budget you want to export and click the Export link. The 

Budget report page opens. 

3. 
Click the Export button and then select Excel to export the entire budget.  

4. 
If you want only certain budget data, such as data for only certain reporting periods or 

departments, set up those filters prior to exporting the budget. This form is also used for the 

Budget report. See Customizing and running the Budget Report (on page 136) in online help 

or download the General Ledger Reports User Guide. 

Modifying a Budget Manually 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Budgets > Budget Details > Budgets`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Budgets`


To modify a budget: 

1. 
Select the Budgets menu option. 

2. 
In the Budgets list, click Budget details for a particular budget. 

3. 
In the row with the account that you want to view, click Budgets. The system displays the 

budget data for this account for each reporting period. 

4. 
In the row with the account that you want to view, click Edit. The Budget amount details 

page shows the details of the account that you are editing, and the dimensions for the 

account. 

5. 
Make changes as needed. 

6. 
Click Save to save your changes. 

Changing a budget does not affect existing transactions. Budget exceeded warnings will occur on 

the new amounts going forward. The system will not alert you to pending A/P invoices that 

exceed the changed budget thresholds.  

---



##### Uploading Changes to a Budget
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 142*

Periodic Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Uploading Changes to a Budget 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Budgets > Export`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Budgets > Export`


At times, circumstances such as an acquisition or the introduction of a new property might 

require you to make significant changes to an existing budget. In this case, you can export an 

existing budget, make changes, and then re-import it. 

1. 
Select the Budgets menu option. 

2. 
In the Budgets list, locate the budget that you want to use as the basis for your new budget, 

and then click the Export link. Exporting to a CSV retains the correct field IDs for upload. 

3. 
Update the CSV file using Microsoft Excel or other spreadsheet program. Spreadsheet 

programs enable you to quickly replicate rows and columns and even automatically change 

the data. 

4. 
After the data in the Excel file is changed, save the file as a CSV file. 
When updating an existing budget, you do not create a new budget ID.

5. 
Click the Import button in the Budgets list to upload the budget data. The system updates 

the existing budget with the new data. 

---



##### Deleting Budgets
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 143*

Chapter 4: Periodic Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Deleting Budgets 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Budgets > Delete`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Budgets > Delete`


It’s important to consider the implications of deleting a budget. After you delete a budget, you 

will no longer be able to use its data to create future budgets, and it can no longer be used in 

financial reports. 

You can delete any budget as long as it’s not being used by a financial report and it’s not the 

default budget. 

• 
A budget that is being used by a financial report can’t be deleted until you modify the 

report and remove the Budget ID. After the budget is no longer tied to the report, it can be 

deleted.  

• 
A default budget cannot be deleted. If you want to delete a budget marked as default, you 

must first make another budget the default budget. 
If you want a warning to appear before you attempt to delete a transaction, select the Warn on 

deletecheck box on the
 My preferences page.

To delete a budget: 

1. 
Select the Budgets menu option. 

2. 
Locate the budget in the Budgets list, and then click the Delete link. 

---



##### Troubleshooting Budget Import Errors
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 144*

Periodic Tasks 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Troubleshooting Budget Import Errors 

There are several reasons why a budget upload might fail. The following are some of the common 

error messages you might encounter, with the steps to resolve them. 

Error 
Steps to resolve 

G/L account not valid 
Verify that you have entered the account number correctly and 

that the account has been created in the General Ledger. 

Duplicate budget 

accounts for a period 

Verify that only a single amount has been entered by period for 

each G/L account/dimension combination. 

For example, if you are budgeting by G/L account only, then you 

cannot have two rows for account 4010. If you are budgeting by 

account and dimension, then you cannot have amounts in two 

rows for the same account/dimension combination. 

Incorrect, not budgetable 

or missing reporting 

periods 

Verify that your reporting periods are correct and that the 

reporting periods are marked as budgetable. 

The reporting periods in your import file must exactly match the 

reporting periods in the system. 

To make a reporting period budgetable, edit the reporting period, 

and then select the Use for budgeting option. 
The dates of periods selected for budgets cannot overlap. For example, you have two reporting periods: one includes January 1 to January 31, and another includes January 15 to February 15. If you select the first to be used for budgeting, then you can’ t 
select the second.

Invalid budget ID 
Verify that the Budget ID has been created and that the value 

entered in the Budget_ID column exactly matches the Budget ID in 

the system. 

---



#### Budget Report
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 145*

Chapter 4: Periodic Tasks 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Budget Report 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > All > Budget`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > All > Budget`


Use the Budget report to analyze your budget information. If you have a financial report with 

budget columns, you can also navigate into the Budget report, and from the Budget report to the 

Budget details page. There you can edit and update budget information, save, and see the 

changes reflected instantly in your financial report.  
The Budget report is a standard report. If you are looking for informa
tion on financial reports, see Creating a Financial Report.

In this report you can: 

• 
Show total budget amounts across all periods.  

• 
Filter on location, department, and other dimensions.  

• 
Display columns for dimension values and select their display order.
