##### Deleting or Inactivating a Vendor
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 347*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Deleting or Inactivating a Vendor 

See the How to Get Here (on page 331) topic for a list of ways to access the Vendors list. 

Deleting a Vendor 

If you haven’t used a vendor for any transactions in the system, you can permanently delete the 

vendor record. 

• 
In the Vendors list, select the Delete check box next to one or more vendor(s), and then 

click Delete to remove the vendor(s). 

Inactivating a Vendor 

Vendors that have already been used can’t be deleted. However, you can make a vendor inactive, 

effectively removing that vendor from drop-down menus and lists throughout the company. 

To make a vendor inactive: 

1. 
Open the Accounts Payable menu. 

2. 
Click the Vendors option. The Vendors list opens. 

3. 
Click the Edit link next to the vendor record you want to make inactive. 

4. 
Change the Status for the vendor to Inactive. 
You can remove unused or duplicate vendors.

---



#### Vendor (Supplier) Information Page
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 348*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Vendor (Supplier) Information Page 

See the How to Get Here (on page 331) topic for a list of ways to access the Vendors (Suppliers) 

list. 

Use this page to add and edit vendor records. A vendor record has six tabs: 

• 
Vendor (Supplier): This tab has two sections, the Summary section, and the Primary contact 

section. Use this tab to record all of the contact details for the primary contact of the 

vendor. 

• 
Additional information: This tab has five sections: Vendor details, Billing details, Email 

template options, Vendor merge, and CA DE 542. Use this tab to record details about the 

vendor and billing, select email templates to associate with a transaction type, view details 

about vendor merges, and indicate when a California form DE 542 is required and note the 

date the form was sent. 

• 
Contact list: This tab has two sections, Pay to/Return to and Contact list. Use this tab to 

define the contact to whom to direct payments and to list all contacts associated with this 

vendor. 

• 
Payment information: This tab has three sections: Payment details, Payment services, and 

Vendor account number. Use this tab to define how and when to pay this vendor. 

• 
Restrictions: This tab has one section. Use this tab to restrict access to this vendor. This tab 

appears only when your system has multiple entities. 

• 
Insurance/License: This tab has three sections for three insurance policies and licenses. Use 

this tab to maintain details about insurance and licenses that cover this vendor. 

---

Vendor Information Page 

Fields 

ID 
The system provides two mutually exclusive ways to add vendor IDs. You 

can type your own unique ID, or you can have the system automatically 

add them from a present numbering scheme. Set up automatic 

numbering for vendors, 

This ID can’t be overwritten after the vendor is created.

Best practices for creating identifiers 

An ID enables your company to keep track of a specific type of 

information, such as G/L account, department, location, transaction, 

vendor, and customer. You can create IDs by adding them on the fly or 

by having the system automatically add them from a preset numbering 

scheme. 

Whichever method you choose, we recommend against using leading 

zeros as part of an ID. IDs with leading zeros can cause errors when you 

import information into your company from Excel because Excel 

removes the leading zeros. 

Best practice: Use a character as a prefix to your ID; for example, C0001 

for a customer ID. 

Workaround: If you must use leading zeros in IDs, then convert the 

column of ID numbers to text format in Excel before importing the file 

content. Excel won’t remove the leading zeros when you save the file. 

Remember to do this every time you use a template to import 

information that requires IDs. 

Name 
The name of the vendor as it will appear on lists and menus across your 

company. 

One-time use (check 

box) 

Designates the vendor as a one-time or occasional use vendor that you 

don’t want to include in your standard vendor lists.

with the vendor.

#### Customers

##### Before You Begin
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 366*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning

Customers 

Use the Customers dimension to create and edit customer records. 

Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

Permissions 

These permissions are required to work with customer records: 

Application 
Activity 
Permissions 

Accounts Receivable 
Customers 
List, View, Add, Edit, Delete 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 
You must be at the top level to add and import customers.

Click

2. 
Click Customers.

Click the Edit or View link of the vendor that you want to view or edit.

 Customers`

2. 
Click

/View`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Cash Management, and then click the All tab. 

2. 
Click Customers. 

3. 
Click the Edit or View link of the vendor that you want to view or edit. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Customers`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Forecasting & Planning, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Customers. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Customers > Edit/View`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Forecasting & Planning, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
Click Customers. 

3. 
Click the Edit or View link of the vendor that you want to view or edit. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Reports > Setup > 
 Customers`

Click

#### Customers List
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 369*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Customers List 

See the How to Get Here (on page 356) topic for a list of ways to access this list. 

Use the Customers list to view and manage customer records. From this list, you can: 

• 
Add new customers. 

• 
Edit or view existing customer details. 

• 
Import customer records. 

• 
Export the list of customers. 

• 
Open a customer’s A/R ledger. 

• 
View the total amount that a customer owes to an entity. 

By default, the Customers list uses icons and indentation to show how parent and child customers 

are related. This hierarchical view is helpful when you have customers that belong to the same 

parent customer, such as when you are doing business with subsidiaries or divisions of the same 

company and need to separately track their contact and billing information. 

The list also provides access to related information, including: 

a) Customer’s A/R ledger: Click the customer’s name to open the customer’s A/R ledger. 

b) Customer totals box: Click the amount in the Total due column to open the Customer 

totals box and view the individual amounts due from the customer.  
If you are working in a multi-entity, multi
-currency company, then a 
View due link appears in 
place of an amount in the Total due column. Click this link to open the 
Customer totals box 
to see the total amount due foreach entity.

---



##### Adding Customers
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 370*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Adding Customers 

See the How to Get Here (on page 356) topic for a list of ways to access the Customers list. 

The Customer information page has multiple tabs that contain basic information about the 

customer, such as company name, address, and phone numbers. 

To add a customer: 

1. 
From the top level, click the Applications menu, and then select Accounts Receivable. 

2. 
Select 
 Add next to Customers. 

Filling In the Customer Section 

To fill in the customer’s company and contact information. 

1. 
In the ID field, type a unique identifier for the customer. 

2. 
In the Name field, type the name of the company as it should appear in lists and menus. 

3. 
Select the One-time use check box to designate the customer as a one-time or occasional 

use customer that you don’t want to include in your standard customer lists. 
These customers won’t appear in the 
Customers list unless you select the 
Include 

one-timecheck box at 
the top of the list.

4. 
Select the Status of the customer record, either Active or Inactive. Inactive customers do not 

appear in any system lists or drop-down lists. 

Filling In the Tax Information Section 
The Tax information
section appears when you use the international version of the RealPage Financial Suite. 

1. 
Select the country where the customer files taxes in the Country drop-down list. 

2. 
In the VAT reg no field, type the customer’s VAT registration number so that you can track 

VAT taxes. 

Filling In the Primary Contact Section 

1. 
Type the name, address, and contact information for the person who is your primary contact 

associated with the company. 

2. 
In the Print as field, type the name of the company as it will appear on payments and 

notices. 

When you create a new customer, the system automatically creates a related contact record 

for you. The Print as field is used in coordination with the Customer ID to create the 

suggested contact name, which is the unique contact ID used throughout the system that 

can’t be edited after creation. This is also the name that appears in all lists and drop-down 

lists. See Contacts for more detailed information. 

---


<!-- Page 371 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Duplicate information is shared between the Customer tab and the Contact list tab. 

Therefore, if you change the data in the Customer tab, the changes are reflected in the 

Contact list tab and vice versa (provided you have not selected the Exclude from the 

company contact list check box). 

3. 
Open the Additional information tab. 

Filling In the Additional Information Tab 

Enter accounting details for this customer: 

1. 
Select the customer’s type in the Type ID drop-down list. 

2. 
In the Sales rep drop-down list, select the user who is responsible for this customer’s 

account. 

3. 
If this customer account is a subsidiary of another customer, click the Parent drop down list, 

and then select the name of the parent company. 

4. 
To use a G/L group other than the default, select it in G/L group drop-down list. 

5. 
In the Territory ID drop-down list, select the sales territory to which you assigned the 

customer. You can also add a territory by clicking the Add link in the drop-down list. 

6. 
In the Attachments drop-down list, add an attachment to associate with the customer 

record. See Adding attachments to a record. Learn more about who can add attachments in 

Permissions for attachments. If you are using Firefox, Chrome, or Edge, you can drop your 

file in the Attachments box to attach the file to this record. 

Filling In the Invoicing Details Section 

1. 
In the Term drop-down list, select the credit terms to extend to the customer. The selected 

term becomes the default term whenever you perform transactions related to the customer. 

You can also add a new term by clicking the Add link and filling in the A/R Terms 

Information box. 

2. 
In the Default revenue account drop-down list, select the G/L account that you want the 

system to use for revenue transactions. When you select an account, the system completes 

the G/L account field with the selected account in revenue transactions, but you can change 

it. 

If your system is an international system, the A/R account drop-down list appears instead.  
To use an alternative A/R account to override the default G/L A/R account, select it in the A/R account drop
-down list. The system populates the A/R override account you select 
here to every invoice or adjustment transaction that includes this customer. See Overriding the G/L account in A/R transactions for more information. In Accounts Receivable, this feature applies to invoices and adjustments only. It doesn
’ t 
apply to recurring invoices, manual deposits, quick check entry, quick invoice entry, or advances.

---


<!-- Page 372 -->
Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

3. 
In the Shipping method drop-down list, select how you will ship goods to the customer. 

You can also add a new shipping method by clicking the Add link and filling in the Shipping 

method information box. 

4. 
If the customer is a reseller of your products and services, type the customer’s resale 

number in the Resale number field. 

5. 
Select the Taxable check box if regular purchases from this customer are taxable. 

6. 
In the Tax group drop-down list, select the contact tax group to associate with the 

customer. This setting applies only when the customer is international. The contact tax group helps the system determine which tax maps and tax details to use to calculate tax for this customer for a
pplicable documents in the Accounts Receivableapplication.

7. 
In the Tax ID field, type the government tax ID number for the customer. (US only) 

8. 
To define a credit limit for this customer, type the amount of credit you are willing to extend 

to the customer in the Credit limit field. 

9. 
To prevent anyone from entering invoices for this customer, select the On Hold check box. 

10. 
In the Statement/Invoice delivery section, select how you want to deliver the customer’s 

statement: 

• 
Print: Mail the customer a printed copy of his invoice. 

• 
Email: Send a copy of the invoice through email. 

• 
Both: Mail the printed copy and send a copy of the A/R invoice through email. 

11. 
In the Default invoice message drop-down list, select the message that you want to include 

in this customer’s invoices. You can also add a standard message by clicking the Add link 

and then filling in the Customer Messages box. 

12. 
In the Comments field, make note of anything that others may need to know about this 

customer. 

13. 
The Currency field appears if your system is configured for multiple currencies. Select the 

currency that the customer uses. 

Filling In the Printed Document Templates Section 

Administrators can set up printed document templates in the Platform Services application. See 

Custom document templates workflow. 

Use this section to select a specific template to use for customer communication with each type 

of transaction: 

• 
A/R invoice (A/R sales invoice) 

• 
Quote

• 
Order 

• 
List 

• 
Invoice

Adjustment

Other 

Filling In the Contact List Tab 

1. 
Open the Contact list tab, and then select the appropriate contact to use for 

communicating with the customer. 

• 
Primary contact 

• 
Bill-to contact 

• 
Ship-to contact 

2. 
In the Contact list section, add categories and contacts to associate them with this customer. 

For example, a customer may have a phone number for the warehouse that you may need 

to call. Enter warehouse for the category and select the contact from the Contact 

drop-down list. 

Refer to Configuring A/R payments for details. 
You must first create contact records before you can select them for these customer contacts. Go to Company > All > Contactsto create contact records.

Filling In the Restrictions Tab 

The Restrictions tab appears when your company has multiple shared entities. 

Use the Restrictions tab to select the restriction level for this customer. The restriction level 

determines which entities can view and access the customer record. 

• 
Unrestricted customer: Visible to all entities at all levels. 

• 
Restricted to top-level only: Visible only to the top-level entity. 

• 
Restricted customer: Activates Location/Location group and Department/department 

group selection boxes.  

Selected locations or departments will be able to view this customer. See Using the 

multi-select box for help on selecting locations and departments. 
When s etting up location groups or department groups, keep in mind that restrictions can be based on groups that contain specific locations or departments, but can
’t be based on 
groups that filter dynamically based on the value of certain fields (such as locations where the status equals “ Active
”). Location groups and department groups that contain dynamic 
filtering won’t appear on the Restrictions tab for you to select.

##### Viewing or Editing a Customer
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 374*



##### Deleting or Inactivating a Customer
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 374*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Viewing or Editing a Customer 

See the How to Get Here (on page 356) topic for a list of ways to access the Customers list. 

The Customers menu item appears on both the 
All and 
Setup tabs of 
Accounts Receivable. Both 
menu options open the same Customers list, and the lists
’settings are exactly the same.

To view or edit customer details: 

1. 
In the Customers list, click the View or Edit next to the customer record. 

The Customer information page opens. 

2. 
View or edit the record as needed, and then click Done or Save. 

Deleting or Inactivating a Customer 

See the How to Get Here (on page 356) topic for a list of ways to access the Customers list. 

If you haven’t used a customer for any transactions in your company, you can click the Delete link 

in the Customers list to remove the customer. 

Customers that have already been used can’t be deleted. However, you can make that customer 

inactive, effectively removing the customer from drop-down menus and lists throughout the 

company. 

To make a customer inactive: 

1. 
Click the Applications menu, and then select Accounts receivable. 

2. 
Select the Setup tab. 

3. 
Select Customers. The Customers list appears. 

4. 
Locate the customer that you want to make inactive and click Edit next to the Customer ID. 

5. 
On the Customer tab, change the Status for the customer to Inactive.  

After you’ve inactivated a customer, you can still access the customer’s record by selecting 

Include inactive at the top of the Customers list. 

6. 
Click the Save button.

#### Customer Information Page
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 375*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Customer Information Page 

See the How to Get Here (on page 356) topic for a list of ways to access the Customers list. 

Use the Customer information page to add and change customer details. From this page, you 

can: 

• 
View or edit customer details. 

• 
Duplicate the customer record. 

• 
Print the customer details. 

• 
Export the customer details in XML format. 

• 
View the audit trail. 

Each customer record has four tabs: 

• 
Customer: This tab contains general information about the customer, such as their unique 

identifier, name, status, primary contact information. 

• 
Additional information: This tab contains additional information about the customer, such 

as their customer type, sales rep, parent company, invoicing details, transaction printing 

options, and email template. 

• 
Contact list: This tab contains the customer’s billing and shipping information. 

• 
Restrictions: This tab allows you to control who can view and edit the customer record. 

---

Fields 

Customers List Columns 

Customer ID

This column contains the state or province where payments and 

statements should be sent. 

ZIP Code

payments and statements should be sent.

This column contains the category assigned to the customer (for 

example, Consultants, Subcontractors, or Equipment). You can search for 

or add a Customer Type ID. The Customer Type ID must exist before you 

can assign it to a customer. 

Total due

company.

Attachments 
An attachment is a bundle of one or more supporting files. It can include 

as many files and combination of file types as you like, such as scanned 

images, spreadsheets, and word-processor documents. You can replace 

or delete an attachment or continue to add or delete files in an 

attachment as needed. 

To reference an attached document, you must first load the scanned 

image, spreadsheet, or word-processor document. You can provide 

permanent, safe storage of supporting documents by uploading them to 

the system as attachments. Learn more about who can add attachments 

in Permissions for attachments. 
If you are using Firefox, Chrome, or Edge, you can drop your file in the Attachments box to attach
the file to this record.

Invoicing Details Section 

Term 
The term designates the credit terms extended to the customer by you, 

such as Due Upon Receipt and Net 30. Either select a Term from the 

drop-down list or click 
 Add to add a new Term. Terms must be 

defined before you can assign one to a customer record. The system 

uses terms to calculate discounts and penalties on customers’ invoices. 

When you define a term for a customer, it becomes the default term 

whenever you perform transactions related to that customer. 

Default revenue 

account/ 

A/R account 

This option appears if you’ve enabled the default G/L A/R account to be 

overridden at transaction entry. The system populates the A/R override 

account you select here to every invoice or adjustment transaction that 

includes this customer. You can override this alternative account as 

needed. 

In Accounts receivable, this feature applies to invoices and adjustments 

only. It doesn’t apply to recurring invoices, manual deposits, quick check 

entry, quick invoice entry, or advances. 

Shipping method 
How you will ship the goods to the customer, such as UPS or FedEx. 

Either select a shipping method from the drop-down list or click 
 Add 

to add a new Shipping method. Shipping Method must exist before you 

can assign it to a customer. 

Resale number 
If your customer is a reseller of your products or services, enter the 

customer’s resale number. Otherwise, skip this box. 

Taxable check box 
Select this check box if regular purchases from this customer are taxable. 

The system adds sales tax whenever an invoice is created for this 

customer.

delivery

Default invoice 

message 

Type a message to print on this customer’s invoices. When you create an 

invoice, the Message field on the invoice is automatically populated with

 Add.

the Customer message box that displays. If you no longer need an 

existing message, first select it, and then make it inactive so it will not be 

displayed. 

Alternatively, you can completely rewrite an unneeded message to make 

it relevant again.

##### Importing Employees
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 383*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Employees 

Use the Employees dimension to create and edit employee records so that you can report on 

employee expense reports. 

Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

These permissions are required to set up area measurement labels. 

Application 
Activity 
Permissions 

Company 
Employees 
List, View, Add, Edit, Delete 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 

How to Get Here 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > Setup > Employees`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Company, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Employees. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Employees`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Forecasting & Planning, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Employees. 

Importing Employees 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Import Data > Set Up Company Master List > 
Employees`


You can import employees’ data by uploading a CSV file. 

---


<!-- Page 384 -->
Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Workflow 

The following illustration shows the steps to download, prepare, and import a CSV file containing 

data. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Import Data > Set Up Company Master List > 
Employees > Template`


1. 
Download the most recent Employees template from the Import data list (on page 419). 

When you get your templates from the Import data list, you get templates that have been 

tailored to your specific company, including the dimensions that your company uses.  

2. 
Open the template file in Excel. 

3. 
Fill in the required columns in the template using the correct formatting as explained in the 

template. See Filling in Import Templates (on page 420). 

• 
EMPLOYEE_ID: Type a unique identifier for the employee. The identifier can be 

alphanumeric and up to 20 characters. 

• 
CONTACT_NAME: Type the employee’s contact name of up to 40 characters. 

• 
PRINT_AS: Type the employee name as you want it to appear on documents. 

4. 
Optionally, add data into the remaining columns. Refer to the Employees Data Template for 

a full list of columns. 

After you’ve added your data, you should then review the data with our best practices in 

mind. This helps eliminate anything that might make your import fail. See Common Errors 

(on page 424) and Best Practices. 

After preparing your data, you are ready to import. 

5. 
Save the file in CSV format. 

6. 
On the Company menu, click Import data. 

7. 
In the Set Up Company Master List section, find the Employees row, and then click the 

Import link. The Employees import box opens. 

8. 
To import the CSV file with employee data: 

• 
Click the Choose file button, and then select the CSV file you want to import. 

• 
In the Date format field, select the desired date format. 

When you select a date format here, the system attempts to import your file using the 

selected date format. If the system can’t import your file with the selected date format, 

---


<!-- Page 385 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

it attempts to discover the date format used in your file and import the data using that 

date format. 

• 
In the File encoding drop-down list, leave this option set to Auto detect unless you’re 

unable to import your file. Depending on your operating system, you may need to 

select a different type of file encoding. 

• 
Select the Process Offline check box to import offline. 

Some imports can take considerable time, depending on their size and how much 

processing the system must do on the uploaded data. If you’re not sure how much 

time your upload will take, select Process Offline. When the system completes the 

process, it sends an email to the address you provide in the Email results to this 

address field. The system also sends you any errors that may have occurred in the 

process. If you prefer to wait until the upload process is complete, do not select the 

Process Offline check box. 

• 
In the Email results to this address field, type your email address. 

This is the email address where the system sends import results and errors. You can 

enter multiple email addresses, separated by a semicolon (;) or comma (,). RealPage 

highly recommends entering an email address. 

9. 
Click the Import button. 

10. 
Resolve any errors. See Resolving errors (on page 424). 

---

##### Adding an Employee
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 387*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Adding an Employee 

See the How to Get Here (on page 373) topic for a list of ways to access this page. 

A basic employee record contains an employee ID and a primary contact name that is used to 

reference the employee throughout the system. 

To add an employee, complete the following fields on the Employee information page: 

1. 
In the Employee ID field, type a unique identifier for the employee. An employee ID can 

consist of numbers, letters, or both. 

You can create employee IDs by adding them manually or by creating a document 

numbering sequence to automatically generate them from a preset numbering scheme. 

As a best practice, do not use leading zeros as part of an ID. IDs with leading zeros can 

cause errors when you import information into your company from Excel because Excel 

removes the leading zeros. 

If you must use leading zeros in your ID, be sure to convert the column of ID numbers to 

text format in Excel before importing the file content so that Excel does not remove the 

leading zeros when you save the file. Remember to do this every time you use a template to 

import information that requires IDs. 

2. 
In the Primary contact name drop-down list, select the contact name that is used to 

reference the employee throughout the system and in printed output. If needed, create a 

new contact. 

The Status drop-down list defaults to Active. Do not change this value to Inactive unless you 

no longer want the employee to appear in Employee drop-down lists throughout the 

system. 

---

*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 388*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Viewing and Editing Employees 

See the How to Get Here (on page 373) topic for a list of ways to access employee records. 

To view an employee’s details: 

1. 
In the Employees list, locate the employee record that you want to view, and then click the 

corresponding View link. 

The Employee information page opens. 

2. 
To view or edit the employee’s contact information, click the Primary contact name link. 

The Contact Information box opens. See Adding a contact for more information. 

3. 
When you are finished, click Done to return to the Employees list. 

To edit an employee’s details: 

1. 
In the Employees list, locate the employee record that you want to edit, and then click the 

corresponding Edit link. 

The Employee information page opens. 

2. 
Make the required changes. 

3. 
Click the Save button. 

---



##### Deleting Employees
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 389*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Deleting Employees 

See the How to Get Here (on page 373) topic for a list of ways to access employee records. 

Deleting an Employee 

You can delete a dimension such as an employee if it hasn’t been associated with a transaction or 

record. 
After you delete an employee record, it cannot be restored.

To delete an employee: 

1. 
In the Employees list, find the employee record that you want to delete, and then select the 

corresponding Delete check box. 

2. 
Click the Delete button. 

A delete confirmation box opens. 

3. 
Click OK. 

Inactivating an Employee 

If you do not want to delete an employee record, but you still want to exclude it from employee 

drop-down lists, you can set its Status to “Inactive.” 

1. 
In the Employees list, find the employee record that you want to delete, and then click the 

corresponding Edit link. 

The Employee information page opens. 

2. 
On the Employee information tab, set the Status field to “Inactive.” 

3. 
Click the Save button. 

---

Employee Information Page 

See the How to Get Here (on page 373) topic for a list of ways to access employee records. 

Use the Employee information page to add, edit, or view the details about a specific employee. 

You can also duplicate an employee record or inactivate an employee so that their record remains 

in the system, but the employee no longer appears in system drop-down lists. 

Fields 

The Employees list includes the following columns: 

Employees List 

Edit (link) 
Click this link to edit the details of an employee record. The Employee 

information page opens. 

View (link) 
Click this link to view the details of an employee record. The Employee 

information page opens. 

Employee ID 
This column displays the employee’s unique ID. 

Last name 
This column displays the employee’s last name. 

First name 
This column displays the employee’s first name. 

Primary contact 

name 

This column displays the contact name that is used to reference the 

employee throughout the system and in printed output. You can change 

this name as needed; for example, if an employee gets divorced and 

changes their last name. 

Print as 
This column displays the contact name for the employee as it will appear 

in printed output. This name can be different from the primary contact 

named used throughout the system. See Adding a contact for more 

information. 

Entity 
This column displays the entity to which the employee record belongs, if 

your company is set up with multiple entities. 

Delete 
This column contains check boxes that you can select to delete 

employees. Select the check box(es), and then click the Delete button to 

delete an employee permanently. This action cannot be undone.

<!-- Page 391 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Employee Information Page 

Employee ID 
This is the employee’s unique identifier. Many companies use the 

employee’s first initial and last name, such as “tsmith.” 

You can assign employee IDs manually or create a document numbering 

sequence to automatically generate them from a preset numbering 

scheme. As a best practice, do not use leading zeros as part of an ID. IDs with leading zeros can cause errors when you import information into your company from Excel because Excel removes th
e leading zeros.

Primary contact 

name 

This is the contact name that is used to reference the employee 

throughout the system and in printed output. You can change this name 

as needed; for example, if an employee gets divorced and changes their 

last name. 

If needed, click the Add link in the drop-down list to create a new contact 

record for the employee. 

Status 
This is the system status of the employee record, either Active or Inactive. 

You can only create A/P invoices and issue payments for active 

employees. An inactive employee does not appear in employee 

drop-down lists throughout the system.

Buttons 

Employees List 

Add 
Opens a blank Employee information page to add a new employee 

record. 

Delete 
Deletes the selected employee(s). 

Done 
Returns to the Company home page. 

Export 
Displays output options for exporting the list of employees. 

Employee Information Page 

Save 
Saves the employee record, including any changes. 

Duplicate 
Duplicates the employee record. You must be in View or Edit mode to 

duplicate an employee. 

Print to 
Generates a PDF file so that you can print it. You can also download the 

information in XML format. This option appears only when you are

changes. This option appears when you open the record with the 

Edit option.  

• 
Table size: Changes the length of the table on the page, if any. 
This command does not affect the size of a table when the table has a fixed number of rows.

#### Departments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 393*



##### Before You Begin
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 393*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Departments 

Although departments are optional, they are useful for filtering and categorizing data in many 

areas of the system. For example, you may want to generate financial reports and graphs based 

on departments. Additionally, many companies set up transaction approvals to be routed to the 

department manager. 

You can create a hierarchy of departments to reflect how departments are organized in your 

company. The hierarchy is established by defining parent and child relationships between 

departments. For example, you could have a parent department called West Coast Division that 

contains several child departments including Engineering, which itself has two additional child 

departments, Software Engineering and Hardware Engineering. 

Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

Permissions 

These permissions are required to work with department dimensions: 

Application 
Activities/Lists 
Permissions 

Company 
Departments 
List, View, Add, Edit, Delete 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 

---



##### How to Get Here
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 394*



##### Importing Departments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 394*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

How to Get Here 

These are the most direct options for accessing department records. You may have additional 

options based on your permission settings.  


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Departments`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Company, and then click the All tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Departments. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Departments`


1. 
In the Applications menu, select Forecasting & Planning, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Departments. 

Importing Departments 

You can import departments by uploading a CSV file. 

Workflow 

The following illustration shows the steps to download, prepare, and import a CSV file. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Import Data > Set Up Company Master List > 
Departments > Template`


1. 
Download the most recent Departments template from the Import data list. When you get 

your templates from the Import data list, you get templates that have been tailored to your 

specific company, including the dimensions that your company uses. See Downloading a 

Template from the Import Data Page (on page 419). 

2. 
Open the template file in Excel. 

3. 
Fill in the import template (on page 420) starting with the required columns in the template: 

• 
DEPT_ID: Type a unique identifier for the department. The identifier can be 

alphanumeric and up to 20 characters. 

• 
DEPT_NAME: Type a department name of up to 40 characters. 

4. 
Optionally, add data into the remaining columns. 

After you’ve added your data, review the data with our best practices in mind. This helps 

eliminate anything that might make your import fail. See Common errors (on page 424) and 

Best practices. 

---


<!-- Page 395 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

After preparing your data, you are ready to import. 

5. 
Save the file in CSV format. File > Export > CSV (Comma delimited file) 

6. 
In the Set Up Company Master List section, find the Departments row, and then click the 

Import link.  

Importing the Data 

To import the CSV file with department data: 

• 
Click the Choose file button, and then select the CSV file you want to import. 

• 
In the Date format field, select the desired date format. 

When you select a date format here, the system attempts to import your file using the 

selected date format. If the system can’t import your file with the selected date format, 

it attempts to discover the date format used in your file and import the data using that 

date format. 

• 
In the File encoding drop-down list, leave this option set to Auto detect unless you’re 

unable to import your file. Depending on your operating system, you may need to 

select a different type of file encoding. 

• 
Select the Process Offline check box to import offline. 

Some imports can take considerable time, depending on their size and how much 

processing the system must do on the uploaded data. If you’re not sure how much 

time your upload will take, select Process Offline. When the system completes the 

process, it sends an email to the address you provide in the Email results to this 

address field. The system also sends you any errors that may have occurred in the 

process. If you prefer to wait until the upload process is complete, do not select the 

Process Offline check box. 

• 
In the Email results to this address field, type your email address. 

• 
This is the email address where the system sends import results and errors. You can 

enter multiple email addresses, separated by a semicolon (;) or comma (,). RealPage 

highly recommends entering an email address. 

• 
Click the Import button. 

• 
Resolve any errors. See Resolving errors (on page 424). 

---

*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 396*

Departments List 

See the How to Get Here (on page 383) topic for a list of ways to access the Departments list. 

Use the Departments list to view a list of the departments in your company. You can sort and 

filter the department list to see only the departments you want. 

If you have long lists of departments in external data files, you can upload them in CSV format on 

the Import data page. You can find more information on uploading CSV files in: 

• 
Preparing CSV files for import 

• 
Import data 

• 
Importing departments (on page 384) 
For users in a multi-entity shared organization, departments can either be common (accessible) 
to all entities, or exclusive (private) to a specific entity. Departments are also a filter object used to restrict user access.

##### Adding and Editing Departments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 397*

Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Adding and Editing Departments 

See the How to Get Here (on page 383) topic for a list of ways to access the Departments list. 

All you need to create a department is to assign an ID and name for it. 

To add a department: 

1. 
Enter an ID and a Name. See Best Practices for Creating Identifiers. 
You can no longer change the ID after creating the department.

2. 
If the department belongs to a hierarchy of departments, select its Parent department from 

the drop-down list. 

3. 
Click the Manager drop-down list to select a manager from the list of employees. If no 


> **Navigation Path:** `names appear in this list, you need to add them by clicking Reports > All > Dimensions >`


Employees. 

If you want department managers to approve their employees’ purchase requests, you must 

select a manager. Otherwise, you can leave this blank. 

4. 
Select the status from the Status drop-down list. The default status is Active. If you no 

longer use the department and do not want it to appear in lists, select Inactive. The 

department information remains in your system, but the system will not display it in any 

department lists. You can view these inactive departments by selecting the Include Inactive 

check box on top of the list. 

5. 
Type a Department title in the Reporting section. 

To save time, you can run a financial report or other General Ledger report for a group of 

entities/locations or departments and have the system automatically split the report into 

multiple individual reports. In this case, the report header for the “individual report” displays 

the name of the department, entity, or location, plus any title text you enter here, instead of 

the company name. 

For example, a location title might provide additional information that you want to display 

on reports, such as the address and manager name. 

San Jose office 

111 Post Street 

Managed by: Karla Grace 

You can enter up to five lines, with a maximum of 50 characters per line. 

6. 
Click the Save button. You can also click the More actions button, and then select Save & 

new. The system saves the record and opens a new blank record. 

To edit a department:

Make the desired changes.

Click the Save button.

See the How to Get Here (on page 383) topic for a list of ways to access the Departments list. 

You can delete a dimension value if it hasn’t been associated with a transaction or record. For

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Locations 

You can create a hierarchy of locations to reflect how locations are organized in your company. 

The hierarchy is established by defining parent and child relationships between locations. See 

Mapping Entities and Locations. 

Before You Begin 

Access can vary depending on your organization’s subscriptions and your permissions. If you do 

not see the option in the menu, then ask your System Administrator to check your permissions. 

The user must have limited or full Administrator privileges to work with locations. 

Permissions 

These permissions are required to work with locations: 

Application 
Activity 
Permissions 

Company 
Locations 
List, View, Add, Edit, Delete 

If your user account doesn’t have the permissions shown above, then ask your System 

Administrator for assistance. If you have administrator privileges, you can assign permissions for a 

new feature to a user or to a role. See Roles and Permissions for a description of how to review a 

user’s permissions. 

---



##### How to Get Here
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 404*



##### Importing Locations
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 404*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

How to Get Here 

These are the most direct options for accessing location records. You may have additional options 

based on your permission settings.  


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Locations`


1. 
Open the Company menu. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Locations. 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Locations`


1. 
Open the Forecasting & Planning menu, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, select Locations. 

Importing Locations 

You can import locations by uploading a CSV file. 

Workflow 

The following illustration shows the steps to download, prepare, and import a CSV file containing 

data. 

Downloading the Template 

When you download templates from the Import data list, you get templates that have been 

tailored to your specific company, including the dimensions that your company uses.  

1. 
Open the Company menu.  

2. 
Click the Import data option. 

3. 
In the Set up company master list section, find the Locations row, and then click the 

Template link. 

The system either downloads the Locations.xls file or prompts you to open or save the file, 

depending on your browser’s settings. 

---


<!-- Page 405 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Filling In the Locations Template 

Add your data using the correct formatting as explained in the template.  

1. 
Open the template in Excel. 

2. 
Fill in the required columns in the template: 

• 
LOCATION_ID: Type a unique identifier for the location. The identifier can be 

alphanumeric and up to 20 characters. 

• 
LOCATION_NAME: Type a location name of up to 40 characters. 

• 
START_DATE: Type the start date related to the location.  

• 
CONTACT_NAME: Type a contact name. This column is required if the contact is 

specified. 

3. 
Optionally, add data into the remaining columns. 

4. 
After you’ve added your data, you should then review the data with our best practices in 

mind. This helps eliminate anything that might make your import fail. See Common errors 

(on page 424), Best practices. 

After preparing your data, you are ready to import. 

5. 
Save the file in CSV format [File > Export > CSV (Comma delimited file)]. 

6. 
Import the location data’s CSV file (Company > All > Import Data > Locations > Import). 

7. 
In the Date format field, unless your dates are already in MM/DD/YYYY format, select a date 

format that you want to use. The system supports 24 date formats.  

8. 
In the File encoding field, leave the setting as auto detect unless you are unable to import 

your file. Depending on your operating system, you may need to select a different type of 

file encoding. 

9. 
Select the Process offline check box if you are importing a large quantity of data or if you 

are unsure how much time your import will take. Some imports can take considerable time, 

depending on their size and how much processing the system must do on the imported 

data. The Process offline feature allows the system to process the data when system activity 

is lighter. If you prefer to wait until the import process is complete, do not select the 

Process offline check box. 

10. 
In the Email results to this address field, type your email address. When the system 

completes the import process, it sends an email to the address with either a message about 

the success or failure of the import, including any errors that occurred in the process. 

11. 
Click the Import button. 
The system does not import the data of any row in the file that starts with a # symbol.

12. 
Resolve any errors. See Resolving errors (on page 424). 

---



#### Locations List
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 406*



##### Adding and Editing Locations
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 406*

Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Locations List 

See the How to Get Here (on page 393) topic for a list of ways to access the Locations list. 

Use the Locations list to view a list of the locations in your company. You can sort and filter the 

locations list to see just the group of locations you want using the standard filters, group filters, 

and custom views. 

From the Locations list, you can: 

• 
Add a new location record. 

• 
Edit and view a location’s details. 

• 
Display the hierarchy of locations. 

• 
Delete a location. 

• 
Export the list of locations. 
If you have long lists of locations in external data files, you can upload them in CSV format on the Import data page. On the Locations row, click the 
Import link.

List Tools 

For information about filters, sorts, and views, see Managing lists. 

For information about field-level controls, see Using controls in drop-down lists. 

Adding and Editing Locations 

See the How to Get Here (on page 393) topic for a list of ways to access the Locations list. 

To add a location: 

1. 
Type the ID and Name of the location as you want it to appear in the system. 

After saving the record you cannot change the location ID.  

If you must change the ID, contact your system administrator and have them run the 

RealPage Accounting Customer Utility tool to change the Location ID/Property ID. 

2. 
If the location belongs to a hierarchy of locations, then select its Parent location. 

• 
For example, suppose that your West Coast Division contains several locations 

including California, Nevada, and Utah. When you define California, Nevada, and Utah, 

select West Coast Division as the parent. 

• 
If you have a multi-entity company that also uses multiple base currencies, then you 

must assign each location to an entity so that the system knows which base currency 

to use for that location. In this case, you can’t change the Parent location. 
You can change the parent of a location only when your system uses one base currency.

---


<!-- Page 407 -->
Chapter 7: Setting Up Dimensions for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

3. 
Type the location’s Tax ID. 

• 
For US companies with domestic-only business, type the location’s state tax ID for 

reference purposes. 

• 
If the location tracks VAT, then type the location’s VAT registration ID. 

4. 
In the Manager drop-down list, select or add the employee who manages this location. This 

field is useful if you route purchasing approvals to the manager of a requesting employee’s 

location. 

5. 
In the Status drop-down list, select Active if you want this item to appear in drop-down 

lists. Select Inactive to prevent the item from appearing in drop-down lists. 

Filling In the Contacts Section 

Unlike contact records that refer to an individual (such as an employee), contact records for 

locations contain information about the location. At a minimum, enter a main contact that 

specifies the location name, address, and other details as they should appear on A/R invoices, A/P 

invoices, and other forms. If the location uses a different address for receiving goods and services, 

then create or select a Ship to contact record.  

1. 
In the Location drop-down list, add or select the main location contact. The location’s 

contact record must contain the company name, address, and other details as you want 

them to appear on A/R invoices, A/P invoices, and other forms. 

2. 
Add or select the Ship to contact record. 

Filling In the Reporting Section 

Enter the information that you want to appear in reports. You can enter up to five lines, with a 

maximum of 50 characters per line. 

To save time, you can run a financial report or other general ledger report for a group of 

entities/locations or departments and have the system automatically split the report into multiple 

individual reports. In this case, the report header for the “individual report” displays the name of 

the department, entity, or location, plus any title text you enter in the Location title field, instead 

of the company name. 

For example, a location title might provide additional information that you want to display in 

reports, such as the address and manager name. You can enter up to five lines, with a maximum 

of 50 characters per line. For more information, see Individual Reports for Each Department 

Location or Entity.

’s configuration page.

Deleting a Location 

See the How to Get Here (on page 393) topic for a list of ways to access the Locations list.

example, if you create “New York” as a value for the Location dimension, and you use it in a line

##### Inactivating a Location

Inactivating a Location 

See the How to Get Here (on page 393) topic for a list of ways to access the Locations list. 

Making a location inactive does not make its associated accounts and transactions inactive. 

Transactions and accounts of inactive locations continue to appear in lists.  

To make a location inactive: 

1. 
Open the Company menu, and then click the All tab of the menu. 

2. 
In the Dimensions section, click Locations. The Locations list opens. 

3. 
Find the location that you want to inactivate, and then click the Edit link. The Location 

information page opens. 

4. 
In the Status drop-down list, click Inactive. 

5. 
Click Save. 

The system continues to include transactions of the location in invoices, adjustments, and 

advances lists. In addition, accounts associated with the location continue to appear in lists. 

However, bank account records cannot be viewed. If you want to view the bank account of 

an inactive location, you must reactivate the location first.

Location Tab

If this location is a child of another location or entity, select the ID of the

This field is required in the case of top-level multi-entity companies that also 

use multiple base currencies. You must assign the location to a specific 

entity, so the system knows which base currency applies to this location. For 

these companies, you cannot change the parent location. 

Tax ID 
Type the tax ID for this location. 

• 
For US companies with domestic-only business, you can enter the 

location’s state tax ID for reference purposes. 

• 
If the location tracks VAT, enter the location’s VAT registration ID. 

Manager 
Select the contact record of the employee who manages this location. 

Assigning a location manager is useful if you plan to route purchasing 

approvals to the location manager of the requesting employee. 

Status 
The default status is “Active.” However, if you are no longer using the 

location and do not want it to appear on lists, choose “Inactive.” This keeps 

the location information in your system and suppresses it from lists without 

deleting it. If you want to see the inactive location later, click the Include 

inactive check box in the list of locations. 

A location: 

• 
Can be made inactive even if the location is referred to by an 

employee. 

• 
Can be made inactive after any location transaction is complete (that 

is, the bill/invoice shows status “paid”). 

• 
Can’t be made inactive if it is referred to by a child location.

#### Segments Overview
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 417*



##### Enabling Segments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 417*

*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 417*

Chapter 8: Setting Up Segments for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Segments Overview 

Segments are custom classifications that help you organize, sort, and report on your company 

information in meaningful ways. Think of a segment as a tag with a set of values that you can 

associate with transactions and G/L entries. For example, you might add a custom segment to 

report on your expense types, employees, or special projects. 

When you enter a new transaction, you can assign a segment to that transaction and then tag it 

with one or more segment values. Segmenting transactions is optional; however, it can be useful 

for identifying transactions and analyzing data in your reports. 

You can define up to 15 segments for your company. An additional segment, called 

Intercompany segment, is available by default. Because it’s a system segment, it cannot be 

modified. 

Enabling Segments 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > General Ledger > Setup > Configuration`


To enable segments: 

1. 
Open the General Ledger menu, and then click the Setup tab. 

2. 
Click the Configuration option. The General Ledger setup page opens. 

3. 
In the Advanced G/L options section, select the Enable segments check box. 

4. 
Click the Save button. 

Reports That Use Segments 

The reports you can run to see your assigned segments include: 

• 
General Ledger Report (on page 109) 

• 
Trial Balance Report (on page 100) 

• 
Financial Reports 

• 
Budget Report (on page 135) 

• 
Segment Activity Report 

---



#### Segments List
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 418*

Setting Up Segments for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Segments List 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Segments`


Use this Segments list to add and manage segments. Segments allow you to organize, sort, and 

report on your data. From this list, you can: 

• 
Add segments, edit, view, and delete segments. 

• 
Import segments in a CSV file. 

• 
Export the list of segments to a file. 

• 
Use the list controls to search, sort, and filter the list of segments. 

By default, the system includes the IC segment in the Segments list when the system is 

configured to create IET transactions automatically. You cannot view the details or edit the IC 

segment, as it is a system segment.

##### Adding Segments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 420*

Setting Up Segments for Forecasting & Planning 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide

Adding Segments 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > 
 Segments`



> **Navigation Path:** `Application > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > 
 Segments`


Use this page to add segment types and their values to the system. After adding the segments to 

the system, you can use them to classify your transactions by assigning segment types and their 

values as you create and edit transactions. You can add up to 15 segments. 
Segments do not appear on the menu unless segments have been enabled on the General Ledger 


> **Navigation Path:** `Setup page. Go to 
General Ledger > Setup > Configuration > Advanced GL Options > Enable`


Segments.

To add a segment: 

1. 
In the Type field, type a two-character alphabetical code to identify the segment. This is the 

code that appears in Segment type drop-down lists when users tag a transaction with a 

segment. 

2. 
In the Name field, type a name for the segment. This name appears in reports. 

3. 
In the Status drop-down list, select Active if you want this item to appear in drop-down 

lists. Select Inactive to prevent the item from appearing in drop-down lists. By default, the 

status is Active. 

4. 
In the Segment values section, type the values to associate with the segment type.  

Alternatively, you can import the segment values (on page 413) from a CSV template. 

5. 
Click the Save button.

##### Importing Segments
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 421*

Chapter 8: Setting Up Segments for Forecasting & Planning 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Importing Segments 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Import Data > Segments > Template/Import`



> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Forecasting & Planning > Setup > Segments > Import`


Downloading the Template 

1. 
On the Import data page, find the Segments row, and click the Template link. 

2. 
Open the downloaded file and fill it with segment data: 

• 
Type: Type a two-character alphabetical code for the segment. 

• 
Name: Type a name that describes the segment. This name appears in reports. 

• 
Segment value: Leave this column blank. 

• 
Status: Type the status of the segment, either “Active” or “Inactive.” 
You can include up to 15 segment types and values in the import file.

3. 
Save the file in CSV format. 

Importing the Segments 

1. 
In the Segments list, click the Import link. 

2. 
Click the Choose file button, and then select the CSV file that you filled with data.  

3. 
In the Date format drop-down list, select a format that matches the date format in your CSV 

file. 

4. 
Leave the File encoding option set to Auto detect unless you’re unable to import your file. 

Depending on your operating system, you may need to select a different type of file 

encoding. 

5. 
Select the Process offline check box to move on to other tasks while a large file uploads in 

the background. The system sends you an email when it completes the upload. 

6. 
In the Email results to this address field, enter your email address to receive a message with 

a list of data import errors or to get notified when the upload is complete. 

7. 
Click the Import button.

### Importing
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 429*



#### The Import Process
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 429*



#### Downloading a Template from the Import Data Page
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 429*

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Importing 

This appendix contains frequently used instructions for importing data into the system. 

The Import Process 

1. 
Downloading a Template from the Import Data Page (on page 419). 

2. 
Filling in import templates (on page 420). 

3. 
Preparing your CSV file for import (on page 420). 

4. 
Importing a budget (on page 423) 

Downloading a Template from the Import Data Page 


> **Navigation Path:** `Applications > Company > All > Import Data`


We recommend downloading a new blank template from the Import data page whenever you 

import information into the system. This is important because:  

• 
Templates are automatically customized based on your company configuration, including 

dimensions and custom fields you created.  

• 
Templates can be updated at any time, especially when a new version of RealPage 

Accounting is released. This means that an older template might not work.  

To download a template:  

1. 
Open the Company menu. 

2. 
Select Import data. The Import data page lists the information that you can import.  

3. 
Find the type of data that you want to import.  

4. 
Click the Template link to download the template. You can find the file in your browser’s 

default download location.  

A prompt opens at the bottom of the web page, asking if you want to Open, Save, or 

Cancel. 

• 
Open: Opens the file in Excel. 

• 
Save: Saves the file to your browser’s default download folder. 

• 
Save as: Saves the file under a new name and in a different location. 

• 
Save and Open: Saves the file to your local computer, and then opens the file in Excel. 

5. 
Click Save. 

Save the template to your computer, and then continue downloading templates as needed until 

you have downloaded all of the templates for data that you want to import. 

A P P E N D I X A 

---



#### Filling In an Import Template with Data
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 430*



#### Preparing Your CSV File for Import
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 430*

Appendices 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

If you have a database administrator (DBA) of your other system, give the downloaded templates 

to your DBA. Ask the DBA to: 

• 
Export the data from your other system, as specified in the import template spreadsheets. 

• 
Put the applicable data into each spreadsheet. 

• 
Save the spreadsheet as a comma-delimited file (CSV). 

If you do not have a DBA, or if your DBA does not have time to complete the whole task, you can 

complete the tasks. Refer to the following topics for guidance on creating importable data. 

Filling In an Import Template with Data 

Perform the following steps for each item that you want to import: 

1. 
Open the template in Excel and review the data structure. The information in the template 

file can help you formulate extraction queries. 

2. 
Export the data from your other source. 

3. 
Copy the exported data into the template file for the same kind of object. Take care to 

exactly match the template’s data structure as you do this. 
Avoid using leading zeros in ID fields. If you must use leading zeros in IDs, convert the column of ID numbers to text format in Excel before importing the file content. Excel does not remove the leading zeros when you save the file. Remember to do this every time you use a template to import information that requires IDs.

4. 
Fill in the required columns. 

5. 
Save the file in CSV format. 

Now that you’ve entered your data and looked through it for any of the common issues imports 

can have, you are ready to import your file to your system.  

Preparing Your CSV File for Import 

The Import data page is a quick way to import your information into the system. However, the 

way you prepare your CSV files directly affects the success or failure of importing your data. Learn 

the CSV import process to streamline the import process. 
This topic provides general information about preparing CSV files for import. Links to information about specific templates can be found in the Import data into the system, which lists all import topics.

---


<!-- Page 431 -->
Appendix A: Importing 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Workflow 

The following illustration shows the steps to download, prepare, and import a CSV file. 

Most imports start with getting the most recent template from the Import data page. When you 

get your templates from this list, you get templates that have been tailored to your specific 

company, including specific dimensions. 

Next you add your data, using the correct formatting as explained in the template. After you’ve 

added your data, you should then review the data with our best practices in mind. This helps 

eliminate anything that might make your import fail. 

After preparing your data, you are ready to import. 

Downloading a Template 

We recommend downloading a new blank template from the Import data page whenever you 

import information into the system. This is important because: 

• 
Templates in the Import data page are automatically customized based on your company 

configuration, including dimensions and custom fields you created. 

• 
Templates can be updated by RealPage at any time, especially when a new version of 

RealPage Accounting is released. This means that an older template might not work. 

To download an import template: 

1. 
Open the Company menu. 

2. 
Click Import data. The Import data page opens with a list of information that you can 

import into the system. 

3. 
Decide on the type of information to upload, and then click the Template link associated 

with that information. 

Other Templates 

RealPage may have additional CSV import templates that are not found on the Import data page. 

Contact your RealPage Support Representative for any templates that have been customized for 

your company. 

Adding Your Data 

After you download the appropriate template, add your data. 

The following example shows a portion of a standard CSV file for importing accounts receivable 

invoices after it has been opened in Excel, but before data has been added. The top row is the

a) Some fields are not required. 

b) Some fields are required. 

c) Header row 

d) Header description row 

Each template contains a set of pre-defined headers, such as BUDGET_ID, ACCT_NO, and soon. 

You enter your data into the blank cells below the header and header description rows without 

changing the headers. 
It is important to read the description for each of the column headers. Some columns are required and if left blank will cause your import to fail.

The following illustration shows a portion of the template after the data has been added. The 

description row has been hidden for this example. 

To add your data: 

1. 
Open the template that you downloaded from the Import data page. 

2. 
Enter your information. Use the header descriptions as a guide for how to enter information 

correctly.

#### Importing a Budget
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 433*

Appendix A: Importing 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

• 
Do not change the headers. 

The header titles in the header row correlate to fields in UI. Incorrect header titles will 

cause an import error. When you download a template, the correct headers are in the 

template. 

• 
Read the header descriptions. 

The header description is the cell under the header row that explains what type of 

information is accepted for each column. If you enter information in the wrong format, 

or with a value not accepted by the column, it will cause an error in your import. 

• 
Enter required information. 

Some data is required, while others are not. Read the description field to determine 

which information is required. If you do not enter the required information, your 

import will fail. 

3. 
After entering your data, save the file as a comma-separated value (CSV) file. When saving 

from Excel, select the comma delimited file type. The file will have the file extension .csv 

after you save it. 

Importing a Budget 

It’s often easiest to create a budget using Microsoft Excel or other spreadsheet program. Creating 

a budget in spreadsheet form allows you to upload large volumes of data. The spreadsheet can 

include all account and dimension combinations. 
For consolidated budgets, RealPage Accounting does not translate your budget from the entity base currency to the consolidated currency. You need to run a global consolidation to do the translation for the budget, and then upload the amount into RealPage Accounting. 

Permissions 

In general, the ability to import data requires permission to access the Import data option of the 

Company application’s Setup tab. This permission is required even if the import is performed 

outside of the Import data page. 

Workflow 

Uploading a budget from a spreadsheet is a multi-step process:  

1. 
Create a new budget in RealPage Accounting and give it a unique Budget ID. 

2. 
Download the budget template from the Import data page. 

3. 
Enter your budget data in the spreadsheet. The rows in your spreadsheet will refer to the 

Budget ID that you created in Step 1. 

4. 
Import the spreadsheet. 
If you are uploading amounts for a consolidated budget, make sure the amounts are already converted into the consolidated currency using your company’s own exchange rate.

## Glossary of Terms
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*



### Accounting period
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*



### Accounts
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*



### Accrual accounting
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*



### Allocation
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*



### Approval policy
*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*

*Source: `RP Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide.pdf`, Page 437*

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Accounting period 

Transactions are recorded and separated into 

different reporting periods such as months, 

quarters, and years. The use of reporting periods 

lets you compare past and present performance. 

Accounts 

An account keeps track of what your company 

owns, owes, spends, or receives. Separate 

accounts let you keep separate records for each 

type of asset, liability, equity, revenue, and 

expense. 

Each account can contain several subaccounts, 

which helps organize and manage your finances. 

Each account tracks the activity of its category. 

For example, the Receivables account tracks 

money coming in and the Payables account 

tracks money going out. Each of these accounts 

typically has many subaccounts. For example, the 

Receivables account may have a subaccount for 

each property: 

1201.001 - Accounts Receivable - Fair Haven 

1201.002 - Accounts Receivable - Coconut Grove 

Each account has a beginning balance. You 

record the increases and decreases for a period, 

and at the end of the period, you have the 

resulting ending balance. 

Accrual accounting 

The accrual method of accounting recognizes 

income in the period earned, and expenses in 

the period incurred. The accrual method is a 

better measure of profit and loss. 

Allocation 

An allocation is a system that automatically 

allocates transactions in a specified ratio to 

multiple dimensions. 

Approval policy 

An approval policy determines who is authorized 

to approve a transaction and defines the 

transaction type that the system sends to 

approvers. 

Approval workbench 

An approval workbench is where users review, 

approve, and decline pending transactions. 

Approval workflow 

An approval policy workflow is one or more 

approval policies that are assigned to a location 

or group of locations. The approval workflow can 

include any combination of purchase order 

policies, job cost approval policies, and A/P 

invoice policies. Workflows can optionally use 

conditions to filter further and control 

transaction evaluations. 

Approval workflows can optionally use 

conditions to filter approval policies further. 

Approver 

An approver is a person responsible for deciding 

to approve or decline a transaction. There are 

three types of approvers: 

• 
General approvers can approve or decline 

only transactions assigned to them or their 

role. 

Glossary of Terms
