Glossary of Terms 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

• 
Unrestricted approvers can approve or 

decline any transaction. 

• 
Delegate approvers can approve or decline 

transactions assigned to another approver. 

Assets 

The tangible and intangible properties used in 

the operation of a business. Tangible assets 

include buildings, equipment, and vehicles. 

Intangible assets include the company’s bank 

account balance and A/R invoices representing 

future income from customers. 

Attachments 

An attachment is a bundle of one or more 

supporting documents, such as spreadsheets, 

scanned images, or word processor documents. 

Budget 

In Job Cost, a budget is an estimate of expected 

costs for a job. 

BVA 

Budget Variance Analysis is an analysis of the 

difference between the amount that you 

budgeted for and the actual amount that you 

spent. 

Cash accounting 

The cash method of accounting recognizes 

revenue in the same reporting period as the cash 

is received, and expenses in the period in which 

cash payments are made. 

Child location 

In the RealPage Financial Suite, properties are 

called locations. Subproperties are called child 

locations. 

An entity represents the corporate office of one 

or more locations, responsible for locations’ 

taxes. Every location is associated with an entity. 

An entity can be responsible for one location or 

a large group of locations and child locations.  

A company can be composed of one entity or 

multiple entities. See also Multi-entity companies 

(on page 431). 

Close account 

A close account is a G/L account used to track 

job completion. 

Commitment 

A commitment, or purchase order, is an 

agreement to purchase goods or services for a 

fixed amount. 

Concession 

A concession is a negative charge awarded to 

the customer as a short-term incentive. Examples 

of concessions include waived fees, reduced rent, 

nuisance discounts, and renovation credits.  

Contra accounts 

A contra account is an account which offsets and 

reduces or offsets the balance of another 

account. You can have contra accounts for both 

assets and liabilities. 

A contra asset account has a credit balance and 

offsets and decreases the debit balance of the 

related asset account. An example of a 

contra-asset account is Accumulated 

Depreciation Autos & Vehicles account, which 

reduces the Automobiles & Vehicles account to 

arrive at the net value.

Glossary of Terms 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

A contra-liability account has a debit balance 

and offsets and decreases the credit balance of 

the related liability account. 

Cost code 

A cost code is a way to track a specific cost 

based on nature or function. 

Cost coding 

Cost coding is a method of grouping individual 

costs based on their nature, function, or work 

phase. 

Credit memo 

A credit memo increases the overall amount you 

owe a vendor. 

Debit memo 

A debit memo decreases the overall amount you 

owe that vendor. 

For example, if you receive a credit memo from a 

vendor, enter it as a debit memo to reduce the 

amount you owe that vendor. 

Debits and credits 

In common use, credit generally has positive 

associations; in accounting, credit is associated 

with decreases in asset, expense, and equity 

accounts. 

Debits increase balances in asset and expense 

accounts and decrease balances in liability 

accounts, revenue accounts, and capital 

accounts. 

Credits increase balances in liability, revenue, 

and capital accounts, and decrease balances in 

asset and expense accounts. 

Debit accounts are asset and expense accounts 

that usually have debit balances; the total debits 

usually exceed the total credits in each debit 

account. 

Credit accounts are revenue (income, gains) 

accounts and liability accounts that usually have 

credit balances. 

Delegate 

The approver who assumes your responsibility 

for approving transactions while you are out of 

the office. Any approvals that have been 

delegated while you are out of the office remain 

with your delegate, even if you turn off the 

out-of-office notification before the delegate has 

approved or declined the transaction. 

Dimension report structures 

Dimension report structures are named 

structures that you can create for the purpose of 

adding custom analysis to the rows or columns 

of a financial report. You can add dimension 

structures for any dimension that you have in 

your company. 

Draw 

In Job Cost & Reserves, a draw summarizes 

invoiced costs by combining job activity into 

predetermined categories. After it’s summarized, 

you can provide the draw to the funding sources 

of the job. 

Draw model 

A draw model is an agreement between a lender 

and your company that lists the budgeted items 

approved for funds.

Glossary of Terms 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

eInvoice 

A system-generated A/P invoice created by 

Spend Management Storefront. 

Entity 

An entity record contains a property’s or 

location’s accounting and legal details, such as 

the business name and address as they should 

appear on A/R invoices, A/P invoices, and tax 

forms. An entity contains the accounting 

information of a location/property or property 

management company. 

In a multi-entity environment, entities are 

distinct—but nearly identical—companies or 

business units that are fully owned by a 

corporate or headquarters entity. Entities can 

represent divisions managed by a common 

entity (central headquarters) using centralized 

payables and receivables and a common chart of 

accounts. 

FAS 

A final account statement (FAS) is a document 

that provides a detailed record of a transaction’s 

activity. 

Funding source 

A funding source is a lender for a job. 

Initial posting date 

Revaluation reports help you manage 

unrecognized gains and losses from the 

fluctuation in exchange rates. Set the initial 

posting date on this report as the end of the 

month. 

Interim financing 

Interim financing, or construction loan, is a 

deployment of capital for short-term 

development, such as the acquisition and 

renovation of properties. This capital is typically 

provided by a lender. 

When the project is complete, owners pay the 

construction loan and replace it with permanent 

financing. 

Job 

A job is a method of tracking the work and 

expenses related to construction at a property. 

Journal entry 

A journal entry documents the movement of 

money. When you add a journal entry, you enter 

an accounting transaction into the records. Every 

journal entry must include at least two equal and 

offsetting entries. This is because every 

transaction involves a change in at least two 

places in the accounting records, and the total of 

all debits and credits must balance. 

Loan schedule 

A loan schedule is a list of all payments 

previously paid (historical purposes only) or due 

to the lender for the loan. 

M-console 

The M-console is the management console used 

by administrators to create and manage multiple 

RealPage Accounting Suite environments. 

M-console access is different from RealPage 

Accounting Suite system access. To access the 

M-console, you must have a separate account in 

the M-console.

Glossary of Terms 

© 2025 RealPage, Inc. 
RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 

Most implementations of RealPage Accounting 

Suite do not need the M-console. 

Multi-entity companies 

In the RealPage Financial Suite, a parent property 

is called a location, and a subproperty is called a 

child location. A location must have an entity 

associated with it. The entity handles the 

accounting and tax activities for its location and 

any child locations. 

In a multi-entity shared corporation, entities 

represent a separate tax identification or a 

separately secured, fully balancing set of books. 

Entities typically represent properties, or 

locations, which are managed by a common 

entity (central headquarters) using centralized 

payables and receivables and a common chart of 

accounts. 

Use the multi-entity console to configure your 

system to support multiple entities. Company > 

Admin > Subscriptions > Multi Entity Console 

> Configure 

See also Entity (on page 430). 

Obfuscate 

The treatment of vendor, customer, employee, 

user, or contract information in one of two ways 

in compliance with the CCPA and GDPR. 

• 
Removal of data so that it is not visible and 

cannot be removed. 

• 
Replacement of data with a xxx 

placeholder. 

oInvoice 

A system-generated A/P invoice created in Full 

Service. 

Overpayment 

When the customer has made a payment for an 

invoice in full, but the payment is more than the 

invoice amount, leaving additional monies to 

apply. 

Permit 

A permit is a category that organizes job 

attachments by type or function. 

PO 

An abbreviation for purchase order (on page 

432). 

Positive Pay 

Positive pay is an automated service that the 

bank offers to help detect fraud by matching a 

company’s check with the check presented for 

payment.  

You can use the RealPage Financial Suite Positive 

Pay feature to generate a Positive Pay file and 

transmit the list of checks to the bank for review. 

The bank compares the amount, check number, 

and account number to ensure that they match. 

Non-matching checks are flagged and sent back 

to the check issuer for review. 

You can also configure the system to submit 

Positive Pay files on a schedule. When a schedule 

is configured, the system generates the Positive 

Pay files containing a list of checks on the 

scheduled dates and automatically sends them 

to the bank. 

Posting 

When you post a transaction, you are 

transferring the balance of the transaction from 

its journal to the General Ledger.

Glossary of Terms 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

Prepayment 

A payment reflecting an invoice that is not yet 

billed. The customer’s account balance is zero. 

Punchout vendor 

Punchout refers to a digital system in which a 

vendor allows customers to browse his product 

catalogs online and then purchase goods or 

services within the client’s e-procurement 

system. 

Purchase order 

A purchase order, or commitment, is an 

agreement to purchase goods or services for a 

fixed amount. 

REIT 

Real Estate Investment Trust 

REMIC 

Real Estate Mortgage Investment Conduit 

Report audience 

A report audience is an audience assigned to a 

report in order to designate who the report is 

intended to be viewed by or addressed to. By 

assigning a report audience to a report, you can 

filter the list of reports by audience type later. 

Revaluate 

An adjustment to a country’s official exchange 

rate relative to the base amount. 

RPA 

An abbreviation for RealPage Accounting. 

Segments 

A segment is a classification option that you can 

use to organize, sort, and report on your 

company information in meaningful ways. It is a 

type of tag with a set of values, which you can 

associate with transactions and G/L entries. 

Sequences 

Document sequences refer to the system’s 

automatic document numbering feature used to 

assign unique identifiers to new records. This 

helps ensure consistency and avoids duplication. 

Automatic numbering, also known as document 

sequencing, is available only for select 

transactions and records in RealPage 

Accounting. Document sequencing uses 

pre-defined numbering plans to assign 

sequential identifiers to new records or 

transactions, such as customer records or A/R 

invoices. Document sequencing is a two-step 

process. First, you configure the numbering 

scheme, then you assign the scheme to the type 

of record or transaction.  

Setup 

The Setup tab appears at the top of most menu 

panes. Click it to display the menu items 

available to you based on your subscriptions, 

user permissions, and admin privileges. If the 

Setup tab does not appear on the menu, then 

you do not have permissions to the tasks on the 

Setup tab. 

Site Experience 

The Site Experience is a scaled-down version of 

Spend Management in a simplified interface. 

While the Spend Management application in the 

RealPage Financial Suite offers more robust 

functionality, most users can accomplish their

Source entity 

The paying entity. The entity that is the source of 

the money in a transaction between two or more 

entities. 

Splits 

A line within a transaction that records the 

account and amount of money involved is called 

a split. 

Statement 

A statement, or Statement of Account, is 

essentially a report. It’s a snapshot that includes 

all open and unapplied transactions at the time 

the snapshot was taken—usually at the end of a 

period. 

Subledger 

A subledger is a ledger containing all of one kind 

of transaction. For example, a subledger may 

contain all accounts receivable transactions, or 

accounts payable transactions, or fixed asset 

transactions. All transactions in all subledgers roll 

up into the general ledger. 

Summary 

Summaries are collections of the same type of 

transactions, grouped together for processing. 

Until you or the system creates a new summary, 

no line items appear in the Summaries list. 

Summaries can be either open or closed. When 

summaries are open in Accounts Receivable, you 

can add invoices, adjustments, and deposits. In 

designing an accounts receivable process, the 

system administrator can give access to the 

pages involved with managing invoices but not 

opening or closing summaries. 

System journal 

A system journal is a journal that is used by the 

system. Users can designate a journal as a 

system journal. Users cannot add journal entries 

to system journals, and system journals are 

excluded from appearing in manual journals 

drop-down lists. 

TIH 

Trust Interest Holder 

Transactions 

A transaction represents money flowing from 

one account to another. Whenever you spend or 

receive money, or transfer money from one 

account to another, it is represented as a 

transaction. 

In double-entry accounting, transactions always 

involve at least two accounts—a source account 

and a destination account. 

Unit 

A unit is a rentable space on a property. 

Unrestricted user 

In the context of approval workflows, an 

unrestricted user can see all transactions that are 

waiting to be approved. Unrestricted users can 

act on these transactions even when the 

approvals are not assigned to them. 

---

Glossary of Terms 

RealPage Accounting Forecasting & Planning User Guide 
© 2025 RealPage, Inc.

WHFIT 

Widely Held Fixed Investment Trust 

WIP account 

A WIP account, or work in process account, is a 

G/L account used to track jobs that are in active 

development. 

Work breakdown structure (WBS) 

A work breakdown structure (WBS) is a system 

that organizes a large project into manageable 

sections. In Job Cost, a job, or project, is 

separated into cost codes that help organize the 

job and track progress. 

Work in process account 

A work in process account, or WIP account, is a 

G/L account used to track jobs that are in active 

development. 

Work paper 

A work paper is an accounting document that 

reconciles all subaccounts of an accounting 

category for auditing purposes. Work papers can 

also be used for preparing financial statements.

About the Forecasting & Planning Application

Forecasting & Planning is a budgeting, planning, and
forecasting solution used to map out an organization's short-
term and long-term financial goals by analyzing past and
present data trends. With this application, you can create,
monitor, and manage budgets or forecast for corporate entities
and then review the current performance of the budget against
the last or current year's budget or actuals.

Setting Up Dimensions

If your company is set up to use dimensions, then all of your
company's dimensions are available in the Forecasting &
Planning application. Any dimensions that you have added
using other applications in RealPage Accounting are included
in Forecasting & Planning. You must have administrator-level
privileges to add and manage dimensions.
To set up new dimensions:

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select the Setup tab.

2. In the Dimensions section, select 
 Add next to the desired
dimension:

▪
Vendors
▪
Customers
▪
Employees
▪
Departments
▪
Locations

3. Fill in the required fields, and then click Save.

Setting Up Segments

If your company has enabled segments, then all defined
segments are available in the Forecasting & Planning
application. Administrators can grant user permissions to use
segments in the Company application.
To enable segments:

1. In General Ledger, select Setup, then select Configuration.
2. In the Advanced G/L options section, select Enable segments.
3. Click Save.

To grant permissions to use segments:

1. In the Company menu, select the Setup tab.
2. Select Users.
3. Find the desired user, and then select View permissions &
roles.
4. Select the Roles tab.
5. Click View under Subscriptions.
6. Click Permissions next to Company.
7. Select the permissions for Segments.
8. Click Save.

To add a segment in Forecasting & Planning:

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select Setup, and then select 
Segments.
2. Type a two-character alphabetical code in the Type field. This
will be the Segment type.
3. Type a Name for the segment.
4. Set the Status to "Active" or "Inactive".
5. Type one or more Values for the segment type or import a
spreadsheet with segment values.
6. Click Save.

Adding a Working Budget

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select 
Working budget.
2. Type a name for the Budget.
3. Select how to create the working budget:

▪
Create a new budget: Creates the working budget from
scratch.
▪
Create a budget from working budget and reporting
period range: Creates the working budget based on an
existing one.
▪
Create a budget from G/L budget and reporting period
range: Creates the working budget based on an existing
G/L budget.

4. If you selected the third option, select the Source year from
which the budget information is coming.
5. In the Create for year drop-down list, select the year for which
you are creating the working budget.
6. In the Start fiscal month and End fiscal month drop-down
lists, select the starting and ending budget periods.
7. If you are creating the budget from scratch, select an Accounts
option:

▪
All accounts: Includes all accounts in the working budget.
▪
Select account group: Creates the working budget with
the accounts in the selected account group(s).

You cannot add or remove accounts to a new working
budget if it is based on another budget.

8. Select the Property (or properties) that you are creating the
working budget for.
9. Click Save.

Viewing the Working Budget Summary

The Working budget summary displays a list of G/L accounts
with the budget amounts and totals for each budget period.

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the budget.
3. In the Filters section, select a Location.
4. Select which accounts to Show:

▪
All accounts: Displays all accounts.
▪
Accounts with budget: Displays all accounts that have
budget data.
▪
Accounts with no budget: Displays all accounts that do
not have budget data.
The list refreshes with the selected filters.

Adding an Account to a Working Budget

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the working budget.
3. Click Add to open a blank Account detail page.
4. Select an Account.
5. Select a Property.
6. To filter the account by dimension, select a Department,
Customer, Vendor, or Employee.
7. If your company has segments enabled, select the desired
Segment type and Segment value.
8. To fill in the Itemization rows section with data from another
budget, select the Budget, and then click Apply.

You can also manually enter items and budget amounts.

9. Type any Comments as needed.
10. Click Save.
11. Click Refresh on the Working budget summary page to
update the account's budget amounts and totals.

Publishing a Working Budget

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the working budget.
3. Click Publish.
4. Select the budget that you want the working budget to Publish
to. The budgets in this list come from the Budgets list.
5. Select the Property (or properties) to associate with the budget.
6. Click Publish.
7. Click Done.

Calculating the Budget Increase for an Account

You can increase budget amounts for an account by having the
system grow each budget period by a specific dollar amount or
percentage.

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the working budget.
3. Click Edit next to the desired account.
4. Select the row(s) you want to change in the Itemization rows
section.
5. Select a Based on option: Amount or Percent.
6. Type the dollar amount or percentage for each budget period to
Grow by.
7. Type the starting amount in the Compute amount field.
8. Click Compute.

Adjusting a Working Budget by Account

You can adjust working budgets in bulk or by individual
accounts.
To adjust in bulk:

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the working budget.
3. Click Adjustment.
4. Select All accounts to adjust all accounts in the working budget
or Select account group to adjust only the accounts included
within the selected account group(s).
5. Select the Location for which you are adjusting the budget.
6. Select the itemization row(s) you are adjusting.
7. Type the Percent adjustment.
8. Click Adjust to increase the amounts for each budget period by
the percentage specified.

To adjust by account:

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click Budget details next to the working budget.
3. Click Edit next to the desired account.
4. Select one or more itemization rows.
5. Type the Percent adjustment.
6. Click Compute to calculate the increase for each budget period.
7. Click Save.

Advancing Year-to-Date Actuals

You can advance actuals through the previous budget period
and report year-to-date actuals in your working budget.

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Working budgets.
2. Click View next to the working budget.
3. Click Advance actuals.
4. Select the budget period that you want to advance Actuals
through.
5. Select the Reporting book, either "Accrual" or "Cash".
6. Select the check box next to one or more properties.
7. Click Advance.

Comparing Budgets in the Workbench

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Workbench.
2. Select a Compare with option:

▪
Budget: Compares the working budget to another budget.
▪
Actual: Compares the working budget to actuals.

3. In the Compare with year field, type the year to compare with.
4. Click Apply.

Adding and Calculating a Payroll

When you add a new payroll to the Payroll list, the system
automatically calculates the payroll for each employee.

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select 
Payroll workbench.
2. Select the Working budget to which you are publishing your
payroll.
3. Select the Entity/location to which the payroll applies.
4. Select one or more Employees to include on the payroll.
5. Click Calculate.
The Status changes from Calculating to Calculated when the
system finishes processing the payroll. Only then can you
publish the payroll to the working budget.

Adding an Employee to a Payroll

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select Payroll
workbench.
2. Click on the Entity/location ID.
3. Click Add employee.
4. Select the Employee(s) to add to the payroll.
5. Click Save.
6. In the Payroll list, click Refresh to recalculate the payroll.

Publishing a Payroll to a Working Budget

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select Payroll
workbench.
2. Click Publish next to the payroll.
3. Click OK to confirm.
4. When the payroll finishes publishing to the working budget, the
Status will change to Published. Go to the Working budget
summary to view the G/L account details for each payroll item.

Calculating Management Fees

You can calculate a management fee to retrieve the budget
amounts for the billed entity for the G/L account in the fee.

The information for each management fee is based on the fee
template set up in Accounts Payable.

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Management fees.
2. Click Calculate next to the fee.
3. Select the Year for which you are calculating.
The budget amounts for the Billed entity populate in the table.
4. Click Calculate.
When the system finishes calculating the fees, you can publish
them to a working budget.

Publishing Management Fees to a Working Budget

1. In Forecasting & Planning, select All, and then select
Management fees.
2. Click Publish next to the fee.
3. Select the Working budget to which you are publishing.
4. Click Publish.
